- **Route staleness — legs re-materialize when the field rebuilds underneath them**: `StepRouted` and the seek-leg constructor stamp `routeFieldStamp = field.BuildStamp` at build; a mid-leg restamp (sensor re-scan interval) now rebuilds the route instead of walking a stale chain.
- **Snap-time clearance — teleports can't embed a body**: `EventPlayer.DebugSnapTo` and `DebugHUDManager.SnapCharacterTo` now verify `TryBodySquare` at the requested spot and recover via `TryNearestFreeSquare` ring scan instead of writing a transform inside collision (the `SeekingCover + mv 0.00` wedge class dies at placement).
- **`ColumnFree` 5 heights — the head-band blind spot closed**: samples were feet 0.06 / waist 0.35h / chest 0.6h — nothing above ~1.08m on a 1.8m body; balcony lips, lintels, sign brackets at head height passed invisibly. Now 0.06 / 0.35h / 0.55h / 0.75h / 0.9h.
- **Debug viz draws the tested envelope**: `PaintFighterBeams` adds a faint `cov_skin` perimeter at `spec + BodySkin` beside the nominal green body square — the drawn box now matches what the mover/cert actually tests (body visibly holds ~6cm off faces — the buffer made visible).
- **`[Yaw]` spam bounded — the debug-paint perf tank**: alternating writers (`seek` vs `swing-p0` vs nudge) each passed the `who != lastYawWriter` fast-path → one `Debug.Log` per frame per fighter, and editor logging is the expensive part. Now a single 4Hz global cadence — writer flaps still legible (the `who` field alternates across lines) at bounded cost.
- **Possession is per-BODY, not global — Tan could never fight while Vinny was possessed** (user: "tan suit won't move even when set to hide or fight" on a blank week): `CombatController.Update` gated the whole world's stand-down on `PossessController.Active` — a global flag — so possessing Vinny ran `ExitCombat()` on EVERY controller every frame. `EnterCombat` still worked (Tan would pick cover, log it, then stand down the same frame). New `PossessController.Body` static carries the possessed VoxelCharacter; three gates now compare `Body == vox`/`Body == character`: the Update stand-down, `TickWatch` reacquisition, and the `beatHoldsReload` freeze. `EventPlayer`'s script-move cancel + facing claim use the same comparison — a script driving Tan isn't killed by Vinny's possession. Combat itself was never week-dependent (`EnterCombatCore` is self-contained, `SetupCoverPit` deliberately sim-free) — the blank week was a coincidence; possessing was the real gate.
- **Structured snag report + diagnostic faces on the shared primitives** (user: "enhance these logs to gather why we snag at the moment teleportation is called"): `ColumnFree` collapsed onto `ColumnFreeDetail` — on failure it names the failed sample (feet/waist/chest) and WHAT it hit (`vol:<id>#<cell>` or terrain) plus the failing sample's world position — walkable-to coordinates of the exact offending voxel. The 9-column square pattern is now one shared `SquareDirs` table iterated by `SquareFree`, `TrySquareStepUp`, and the new `ExplainSquare` — verdict, climb probe, and diagnostic can't drift. `BuildUnstuckReport` emits one block at teleport: pos→free + escape distance, input delta, body spec, the re-probed square at the blocked destination (which sample refused + which volume), the step-up verdict, the field's own walkable/reach opinion of both cells (green+reachable wedge = the map lied; off-window reports distance outside, not a dead "cell[-1]"), the blocked destination's real ground height, zone/combat/duck snapshot. First slice decoded: stoop wedge at `block_26_building` frontage — 5 feet-height failures in building voxels, step offered at band edge but raised square still hit the facade, and he was OFF the field window entirely (cell -1). This is the seed of the per-character body-debug emit the user wants to extend (health/movement/tracking).
- **Possess unstuck teleport** (user: "can you teleport the possessed mode nearby if this condition is met"): `MoveWithCollision` now tracks `stuckT` — every lane (combined + both slides) refused while input is held accumulates; at 0.75s it calls the new `VoxelCollisionWorld.TryNearestFreeSquare` — an 8-direction ring scan (0.25m rings out to 2m) that probes each candidate's column top (must land within ±1.5m — a roof or pit is a different place, not an escape) and verifies the identical skinned `SquareFree`. Teleports to the first hit, logs `[Possess] unstuck → (x,z)`, retries probe every 0.2s if still wedged. Scoped to possess on purpose: routed actors can't reach the state (edges are transition-certified); this is for pockets only a player can steer into.
- **`EdgeOpen` certifies the transition, not just the endpoints** (user: residual snag at tenement-front complex geometry — "the only time it will snag someone" is a flowfield-routed ped): flood edges previously checked only `|ΔgroundY| ≤ stepUp` between two walkable anchors — the leg between certified cell centers was never swept, so a pillar edge/seam pinch between cells could be routed but not executed. Now: real midpoint ground via `TryColumnTop`, gated within step of BOTH anchors (a mid-leg wall can't be reached, a mid-leg gutter can't be climbed out of — both honestly closed), then the skinned `SquareFree` at that point — shared-edge center for cardinals, the 4-cell vertex for diagonals. A certified edge ≡ an executable leg; peds on flowfields can't be routed into a snag the body can't take.
- **`BodySkin = 0.06f` collision buffer — seam snags prevented, not recovered** (user: stuck in volume seams along the tenement block; rejected a nudger in favor of a buffer): `SquareFree`'s perimeter samples now sit at `half + BodySkin − 0.03`, so the tested envelope runs ~6cm wider than the spec'd square and the body can never press flush against a face. Sub-skin discontinuities (volume-seam steps, half-cell lips, recess wedges) live inside the skin zone the tested square never enters — wall-following slides OVER them. `TrySquareStepUp` probes the same inflated 9, and `CoverField.FootprintOK` certifies `bodyHalfMeters + BodySkin` — certified envelope ≡ executed envelope, no map-promises-more-than-body fork. Visible consequence: the green box now holds ~6cm off wall faces — that gap IS the buffer. First-guess value ≈ half a 0.1m terrain voxel; if a seam still snags, it wants 0.12 (full cell).
- **`SquareFree` 5→9 samples — edge-midpoint columns close the nose-in blind spot** (user: square clipped deep into cover objects when "nosing" into them): the square tested center + 4 inset corners at ±(half−0.03) — nothing along each edge's middle. A wall CORNER or yawed face slid between the two corner columns and the square advanced until the center column (a full `half` behind the edge) hit — penetration up to ~half the footprint. Added the 4 edge-midpoint columns at (±off,0)/(0,±off); `TrySquareStepUp` probes the same 9 so step-up offers exactly what the square tests. Flat-face bound stays ~0.03 inset + cell snap; the corner/yaw case drops from ~`half` to the same. A razor post threading the ~0.11m lateral sample gaps remains a shared blind spot — map and mover see it identically.
- **Feet/cert squares paint at window level — corpse-beam fix** (user: "adjusting body footprint breaks the square — stops following, doesn't resize"): `PaintFighterBeams` was called only inside `DrawCombatTab` → `c.lots` fold. A beam is a persistent dict entry that only moves when `SetCustomBeam` runs again — so on any other tab (e.g. COVER, where the footprint slider lives) the square was a frozen corpse from the last Combat-tab paint. Calls moved to the window draw after `EndScrollView` — the body square is a physical spec, not panel content; it now tracks/resizes on every tab.
- **`stepUpMax` default → 0.15f for all characters** (user feel-tested 0.15: smooth over tiny debris, walls stay walls): `VoxelCharacter.stepUpMax` field default plus both fallbacks (`CombatController` property, `CoverSensor` stamp) moved together — the step law stays single-valued.
- **`stepUpMax` moved onto `VoxelCharacter` — one slider, one step law** (user: "i need another slider to adjust step up height"): the step limit existed as TWO serialized copies — `CombatController.stepUpMax` (public, fed the flood + footprint + mover) and `PossessController.stepUpMax` (private, tooltip literally said "mirrors CombatController.stepUpMax"). Now single-owned on `VoxelCharacter.stepUpMax` (0.45 default): `CombatController.stepUpMax` is a delegating property so `CoverSensor`'s `field.bodyStepUp` stamp and all internal reads keep working; possess reads `vinny.stepUpMax`. New `step up m` slider under `body footprint m` on both COVER + TAN tabs — dragging it moves the flood's `EdgeOpen` gate, the square's deck rule, and both movers' climb probes together, on the next field build. Headless `csc` clean.
- **Domain collapse — "unreachable IS unwalkable"** (user: "the low wall SHOULD be unwalkable AND unreachable — make this one and the same"): consumers already gated on `map.Reaches`, but mode-8 painted the pre-collapse verdict — green included flood-islands. Now the domain is the composed verdict `Walkable ∧ meMap.Reaches` and the paint reads it: **green** = fits ∧ flood-served on preferred floor · **olive** = in-domain, unstamped material · **amber (`walk-island`)** = physically legal but the flood can't serve it — a sealed pocket or too-tall ledge top ("can stand, can't get there") · **red** = physically impossible · **clear** = no ground. `SwingSideScore` upgraded to `MeMap().Reaches(di)` — a peek he can't route to is rejected at pick time instead of mid-leg. Idle/no-map falls back to the physical domain. Caveat: "unreachable" is relative to the feet seed and the 16m field window — a pocket connected only via outside-the-window routing reads as an island (bounded by the field, same as every verdict). HUD mode-8 legend updated. Headless `csc` clean.
- **Disagreement audit — two more forks collapsed onto the shared primitives** (user: "do we have any other systems that disagree"): (a) `WalkLayer.ProbeFit` re-implemented the 3-sample column in a second copy (its own comment claimed single-ownership while duplicating it) — now delegates to `world.ColumnFree`, so the cache stores the shared law's answer, not a parallel derivation; `IsFree` helper retired. (b) `CorridorFree` swept only 3 perpendicular columns while the mover tests 5 — a post on the square's forward edge slid between the samples: the pull certified, the mover refused mid-leg. Now sweeps `world.SquareFree(p, BodySqHalf, h)` per interpolation step — a certified corridor ≡ an executable path, exactly. Remaining named seams: crouch doesn't shrink the collision column (posture vs occupancy — defensible), `EventPlayer` placement bypasses SquareFree (authority seam, not a verdict), razor slivers between sample points (identical blind spot in map and mover — they fail together or not at all). Headless `csc` clean.
- **Step-up now probes the whole body square — debris/curb edges climb** (playtest: "every tiny thing snags us instead of walking smoothly over it"): when `SquareFree` made the square the test, the step-up probe still read only the *center* column top — the square's leading edge hit thin debris → move refused → the center probe read the ground BEYOND the chunk → no climb offered → snag (a regression the old point-column never had: it failed only when the center itself hit the chunk, exactly where the probe looked). New shared primitive `VoxelCollisionWorld.TrySquareStepUp(feetCenter, half, stepUpMax)` — column tops at the square's own 5-sample pattern, the highest top in (feet+0.04, feet+stepUp] wins, caller re-verifies the raised square (a wall's out-of-band top still walls). Both `TryMove`s (possess + combat) rewired — the leading edge of the square now offers its step, not just the center's. Headless `csc` clean.
- **Feet square was a stale beam — hoisted out of the TEMPO fold** (playtest: "my body square isn't growing or shrinking as i move body footprint"): `PaintFighterBeams` ran inside the `TEMPO — beat lengths` SecFold — collapsing the section stopped the draw while the old `SetCustomBeam` geometry persisted, so the on-screen square froze at whatever value it last painted (a dead beam, not a live spec read). Both fighters' `PaintFighterBeams` calls now run unconditionally when the pit block exists — the body square is a physical spec, always drawn. `pitTanCC` hoisted alongside `scc` for the callsite. Headless `csc` clean.
- **Footprint slider displays truth + auto measures the hip band, not the chest** (playtest: HUD showed slider `0.00` while `walk cert` read 0.76m — the panel displayed the raw `footprintOverride` field while the resolved spec lived on a separate line, an unreadable mapping; user had to jiggle the slider to "break the staleness"): both `body footprint` sliders (COVER + TAN) now display and edit the **resolved `BodyFootprint`** — the number shown is the number the body uses, with the label switching `(auto)`/`(set)`; drag-to-0 returns to auto. The measure itself also moved: the 20–70% chest band caught the full arm/shoulder span (0.76m) — now the **10–50% hip band** (below arm spread, the part of the body that must physically fit a doorway), percentile-trimmed (90% core — hand tips and props can't widen the column). Log reports per-axis extents so an inflated axis names itself. Headless `csc` clean.
- **Auto body footprint now measures the torso band, not the render bounds** (playtest: possess mode blocked on open sidewalk at default — `BodyFootprint` auto was `max(WorldSize.x,z)` = 0.96m, the model's full bbox including pose spread — any nonzero override unlocked the sidewalk, proving the auto path itself was broken): `MeasureTorsoFootprint` scans the loaded `voxelData` in the 20–70% height band (hips→chest) and takes the max XZ extent — the column the world must actually admit. Feet splay, hat brims, arm spread stop inflating the spec; a fatter model still measures fatter. Override unchanged — `footprintOverride > 0` wins; logs the resolved value at load. Headless `csc` clean.
- **"Can" is physics, "should" is routing — ONE occupancy primitive, every consumer** (user: "cant and shouldnt shouldnt even exist — if we can, we should"): the audit found FOUR competing body-space implementations that could never agree — `CoverField` cell verdicts + overlap stencils, `CombatController.MoveWithCollision` sweeping `vox.WorldSize` (the full model bounds — **the footprint slider literally could not change what a body collides with**), `CombatController.CorridorFree` sweeping `BodySqHalf`, and `PossessController` testing a zero-width *point column* at center (the narrowest test of all — why WASD fit through gaps the map rejected). All collapse onto two new primitives on `VoxelCollisionWorld`: `ColumnFree(feetCenter, bodyH)` (feet+0.06/waist 0.35h/chest 0.6h — identical sampling everywhere) and `SquareFree(feetCenter, half, bodyH)` (center + 4 inset corners — "can this body stand centered here"). Rewired: field `ColumnFits`/`ProbeFloor` → `ColumnFree`; combat mover `Free` → `SquareFree(corner+modelHalf, BodySqHalf, dims.y)` — the spec'd square, not render bounds; `CorridorFree`'s 3-column sweep → `ColumnFree`; possess `Free` → `SquareFree` — **possession now collides with the identical BodyFootprint square the map certifies** (the slider finally gates WASD too). **The material mask is no longer a gate**: `Walkable`/`FootprintOK`/`OverlapClear`/`ProbeFloor`/`NearestWalkable` are pure physics; `walkableMaterials`/`WalkMask` survives as the *preferred floor set* fed to `BuildDistMap`, where edges into unstamped cells cost `UnstampedCost`×2.0 — designation steers, never bans ("if we can, we should"). Debug V-mode walkable is now a three-verdict physical read: **green** = body fits on preferred floor · **olive** = physically legal but unstamped ("can, not preferred" — cost, not ban) · **red** = physically impossible, the identical verdict the movers run · **clear** = no ground. Reject tally splits honest categories: `walk-fit` (center column blocked), `walk-square` (body's reach into a covered cell failed), `walk-unstamped mat N` (informational — legal-but-unpreferred anchor count). `walkMaskCached` and all mask-typed `Walkable`/`NearestWalkable` signatures gone. Headless `csc` clean.
- **Continuous body certification — the map now asks the SAME question the mover asks** (user: "the problem is the lack of consistency — our character can't snap in as a natural fit"): the K×K block test quantized the body into cells (0.6m body → 0.75m cert → 25% phantom reach + cell-boundary aliasing = the "not snug" green). `bodyHalfCells`/`HalfCellsFor` are gone; `field.bodyHalfMeters` (stamped from `vc.BodyFootprint`) drives a **continuous** `FootprintOK`: the square's exact cell span comes from `FloorToInt` on the true extents; covered cells that pass their own verdict are trusted whole, and each FAILED covered cell is re-asked at the square's true reach — `OverlapClear` probes the cell∩square rect's 4 corners + center at voxel-exact positions (ground cast, material, deck delta, the same 3-sample fit column). A wall in a cell's far half now fails only anchors whose bodies actually reach it — erosion lands at exactly half a body, aligned to faces not cell edges. `Walkable` verdicts are now cached per cell per build (WalkOk/WalkBad flag bits, mask-guarded) since the flood re-asks up to 8× per cell. Reject tally is now three-way: `walk-mat N` (banned material) / `walk-fit` (column blocked) / `walk-square` (mat+fit legal, body's reach failed). `DebugCertMeters` now reads `BodySq` — cert ≡ body, the amber outline self-hides. Known bound: both map and mover sample — a razor sliver between stencil points shares the identical blind spot in both (agreement, not perfection). Headless `csc` clean.
- **Feet marker is now the REAL body square — and the cert square** (user: "convert the green X into square boxes that actually represent the size we are using so i can see the size grow and shrink"): `PaintFighterBeams`' two crossed 0.24m beams replaced by a 4-edge green perimeter at `ch.BodyFootprint` — the exact column the mover sweeps and the field certifies, resizing live with `footprintOverride`. A second amber perimeter draws the **cert square** (`cc.DebugCertMeters` = `bodyHalfCells×2+1` cells) when it's larger than the body — the visible gap between the two outlines IS the cell-rounding padding; at a commensurate cell size (0.2m cells / res 80 with a 0.6m body) they collapse onto each other. Feet/cert boxes draw in any state (physical spec is always true — tuneable at SETUP before the duel); the dst post + relocate beam stay gated on combat. Headless `csc` clean.
- **Body-square spec moved onto the character — `VoxelCharacter.footprintOverride` (0 = auto)** (user: "i need a way to reduce worldsize or set it specifically vs being computed"): the body footprint was a sensor diagnostic (`CoverSensor.footprintMeters`) layered over measured `WorldSize`, with the mover's `max()` floor forbidding any shrink below the bbox — three layers deep and impossible to genuinely reduce. Now the character owns the spec: `footprintOverride` (serialized, inspector-settable) → `BodyFootprint` resolves `override > 0 ? override : max(WorldSize.x, WorldSize.z)`. `CoverSensor` stamps it into the field; `CombatController.BodySq` reads the identical property — **the override is authoritative for the mover too** (the old floor is gone: setting below measured genuinely shrinks the certified column — declaring the torso column, not the model bounds). HUD `body footprint m` sliders (COVER + TAN) now write `vc.footprintOverride` — same field the inspector exposes, so the dragged value IS the spec. For spawned characters the field is runtime-default 0 (auto); bake a value by setting `vc.footprintOverride` at spawn or promote it into the character asset later. Headless `csc` clean.
- **`WalkLayer` — the world's walkability is now ONE persistent, event-driven cache** (user: "why are we probing at all? isn't a cached state of the map itself more efficient — this aligns with my event-driven architecture"): new world-fixed verdict grid owned by `VoxelCollisionWorld` (`world.Walk`), lazily-allocated 64×64-cell blocks (16m²) so it only pays for queried regions. Per world-cell: `groundY`/`floorMat`/`Grounded`/`Fit`(cached at `fitH`, re-probed on body-height change)/`probeY` (deck band, re-probed on >0.75m deck shift — roof-vs-street last-writer, bounded). `CoverField.Build` calls `FillWindow` — a 4096-cell **copy** instead of per-cell re-derivation; every consumer reads the same arrays (zero signature churn), and a cell-size mismatch falls back to the old lazy probes untouched. **Invalidation is event-driven at the write funnel**: `UploadVoxelEdits` decodes the dirty index span → tight world AABB → `Walk.Invalidate` (blasts, slumps, plan playback all covered); `RegisterTerrain(Chunk)`/`RegisterVolume`/`UnregisterVolume`/`ClearVolumes` invalidate their footprints. Rescan cadence still gates *consumption*, but the truth updates instantly on write — no more probe-and-hope. HUD readout: `walk layer: N blocks · M cells cached · K invalidated`. Headless `csc` clean.
- **The mask now binds the WHOLE square — labels specify the domain exactly** (user: "why am i hand tuning Walkable vs Not when the SIDEWALK material is supposed to specifically detail this exactly — i dont even need a slider if we specify this exactly everything collapses perfectly"): `FootprintOK` previously demanded covered cells be merely *physically* passable — designation bound only the anchor, producing the fuzzy hand-tunable fringe where anchors could drape their square over unstamped solid. Now every covered cell must be `mat ∈ mask ∧ grounded ∧ fit ∧ deck-compatible`: the domain = painted region **eroded by exactly half the body square** — hard, binary, deterministic. Painting IS the spec; `footprintMeters` stays as diagnostic override only. **Hard color boundaries**: `fieldTexArr` was Bilinear — interpolating between cells fabricated half-verdicts at seams (the pink halos). Now Point — 1 texel = 1 cell (res is pinned to 64), each tile paints its own verdict with crisp borders; modes 4–6 go per-cell blocky too, which is the honest read (each cell HAS one score). Headless `csc` clean.
- **Live footprint slider — tune the walkable dead-band against real geometry** (user: "i can fine tune it to the geometry visually"): `CoverSensor.footprintMeters` (0 = auto from `WorldSize`) feeds `HalfCellsFor` at every field rebuild — the HUD `body footprint m` slider (COVER + TAN tabs, per-fighter like every other sensor tune) re-stamps `bodyHalfCells` on the next scan and the V-mode-8 tiles re-paint live. `CombatController.BodySq` = `max(measured, footprintMeters)` — a widened slider moves map AND mover in lockstep; a narrower one only loosens the map (deliberate diagnostic latitude — the body can't physically shrink). Readout under the slider shows the live cert: `K×K cells ≈ m square · dead-band m`. Headless `csc` clean.
- **One body-square, one predicate — "walkable" now means the whole footprint fits** (playtest: swapped-side Tan ground at a wall-end exactly like Vinny — "are we sure he can fit through all openings?" + user's gameboard doctrine: "the Dijkstra map, the character size, and the sidewalk material all need to agree on the size and shape of a footprint"): audit confirmed "walkable" had THREE different definitions — `ColumnFits` point column at cell center, `CorridorFree` zero-width line, `MoveWithCollision.Free` point column — nothing modeled horizontal extent, so the flood promised 1-2-cell slots a real body can't take. Now `CoverField.bodyHalfCells` (stamped per-build by the sensor from `VoxelCharacter.WorldSize`) turns `Walkable` into: anchor = domain verdict (Grounded ∧ Fits ∧ mat∈mask) ∧ the (2h+1)² block physically passable at a deck within `bodyStepUp` — the square may straddle a curb, never a ledge, never a wall cell, never off-field. `HalfCellsFor` picks the smallest ODD K covering `max(WorldSize.x,z)` — 0.6m body at 0.25m cells → K=3, a 0.75m cert square. **Every consumer inherits it through the same `Walkable` signature** — flood, EdgeOpen, NearestWalkable, picks, swing dests, the V-mode tiles (the eroded domain IS the gameboard now). `CorridorFree` upgraded to the swept square (center + 2 edge columns × 3 heights); `MoveWithCollision.Free` to the square's center + 4 inset corners — the executor answers the same square the map certifies. Consequences: stands shift one cell out from walls (the "flush" cells were never hostable — the center can't be closer than half the square), narrow slots leave the domain *structurally*, and a map-certified route can't ask the body to fit where it can't. `bodyHalfCells=0` preserves legacy point semantics. Residual caveat logged: per-cell `Passable` still probes the cell CENTER — a razor wall inside a cell's far corner can evade it (sub-cell blind spot; the mover's corner pattern catches more — corner-augmented `Passable` is the refinement if wedges persist). Headless `csc` clean.
- **Snap voids stale script claims — the weekly-order slerp was overriding pit facing** (playtest: "using swap sides doesnt face vinny correctly" — user-diagnosed cross-contamination, confirmed): `EventPlayer`'s `hasTargetRotation` slerp writes `transform.rotation` **every frame while `running`** — armed by weekly-order `FaceTarget`/`StartMove` events, voided only on combat non-Idle. At SETUP PIT Vinny is Idle → a live script claim slerped the snap's facing back within ~a second (gotcha #12's fix made the snap instant but never voided *pre-existing* claims — "an authoritative override must void the channel, not just write through it"). Fix completes the doctrine: `DebugSnapTo` now voids `hasTargetRotation` **and** `currentMoveEvent` (a live lerp would yank position to the event endpoint the same way); the slerp and the move lerp both also die on `PossessController.Active` — the player is an owner too. `SnapCharacterTo` (Tan) unaffected — no EventPlayer drives him. Gotcha #12 postscript added. Headless `csc` clean.
- **Walk-domain V-modes — the tile grid paints ON THE WORLD now** (playtest: "walkable is ONLY visible through a ray beam — it should be on the world"): the `Sprites/Default` ground quad is depth-tested against raymarched depth → occluded by every voxel surface (same failure the vision wedge had — fixed there by mode 7, same fix here). New `IterVisMode.CoverWalkable` (8) / `CoverReachable` (9): in those modes each sensor bakes its slice as **display colors** — `Bake(layer, mask, bodyH, meMap)` with the fighter's own designation — into `_CoverFieldTexArr`, and the raymarch outputs them alpha-weighted on every hit surface at its XZ footprint. Result: green/red/amber **tiles land on the floor, wall faces, beam proxies, curbs — the world itself** — not a floating quad. Union = "any fighter's domain" (max-alpha wins, matching the 2-lights overlapping rule). `Reachable` with no map (idle/pre-combat) falls back to the walkable read — amber strictly means "a real flood declared unreachable". V-cycle mod 10; HUD banners label both modes. Shader edit: mode-7 gate narrowed to `6.5<_IterVis<7.5` so 8/9 reach the cover-field branch; rank = `fs.a` for display slices. The `ground-quad field` HUD toggle stays as a fallback for non-raymarched views. Headless `csc` clean.
- **Duel parity audit + `swap sides` toggle** (playtest: "Tan Suit has perfect execution while Vinny gets stuck — compare"): audited every asymmetry candidate — `AttachCombatStack` gives both fighters the identical component stack + mask fill, `StartPitDuel` calls the same `EnterCombat(threat, body)`, `holdInCover` clears in Fight mode, `CombatController` has zero name/identity branches, arena interior is mirrored N↔S (`VALLEY`/`FLANKS`/`PILLARS`/`PLANTERS`). Conclusion: **no code/config asymmetry exists** — the divergence is emergent: the unrouted swing step-out defect (fixed via `StepRouted`) hit whoever's peek needed a corner-rounding step; position gives each fighter a different field footprint; `failedFaces`/`searchVisited`/side rolls accumulate per-fighter; Vinny is the inspected one so his stumbles read first. New `pitSwapSides` toggle in the pit section — SETUP PIT with it on puts Vinny NORTH + Tan SOUTH: if the stumble follows the *side* it's positional (field/geometry), if it follows *Vinny* it's per-instance state.
- **Walkable/Reachable debug layers on the field heat quad** (user request — "see the map walkable grid the way our characters see them"): `CoverField.Layer` gains `Walkable` (green = in the actor's declared domain — grounded ∧ body fits ∧ stamped floor · red = solid ground outside the domain — wall tops, unmasked materials, "floor there, not for you" · clear = no ground) and `Reachable` (bright→dark heat by real corridor cost from his feet · **amber = the fenced diagnosis: designation says floor, flood says no corridor** — a sealed pocket reads painted-but-unreachable). `Bake` takes the actor's mask/body/map as params — the field holds channels, the controller owns designation (`DebugWalkMask`/`DebugBodyH`/`DebugMeMap` read surface). `DrawHeat` drops the threat requirement for these layers (the domain exists with or without a fight). HUD layer picker picks them up automatically — COVER tab → `ground-quad field` → `Walkable`/`Reachable`. Headless `csc` clean.
- **Every point move now consumes the map corridor — `StepRouted` + the post-combat watch (collapse gaps, playtest 2026-10-22)**: two doctrine holes found in the same duel. (a) *Swing step-outs and returns never consumed the `DistMap`* — `swingDest` was verified `Reaches` as a verdict but the path walked a bare `StepToward` line, so a step-out at a wall END face clipped the corner column and ground `swing-p0`/nudges/return-stalls (bug `SWING_STEP_OUT_CORNER_CLIP`). Now `StepRoute(who, finalDest)` is parameterized and `StepRouted` materializes `BuildRoute`'s chain once per leg (`swingP0Built`/`swingP2Built`/`returnBuilt` latches) for `swing-p0`, `swing-p2`, and the Duck `return` leg — open ground still pulls to the same straight line; it deviates only where a corner genuinely blocks. Bare-line fallback kept for legitimately unroutable dests (out-of-field rims), logged once. (b) *Combat exit was deaf AND blind* — `ExitCombat` went to `Idle`, `Update` returned early, `OnShotHeard` discarded reports at Idle: an opponent re-appearing after a "flank clear" verdict could never re-engage. Now `ExitCombat(keepWatch)` splits organic endings (orbit verdict, blind abandon, unresumable leg — all pass `true`) from manual/possess/teardown exits (`false` — stand ALL the way down). The watch stashes `watchTarget` and `TickWatch` runs at Idle on the `visInterval` cadence: silhouette probe ∧ posed-cone coverage — the SAME detection law as combat — re-enters `EnterCombat` where he actually stands; `OnShotHeard` at Idle+watch re-enters at the heard position. The duel loops until somebody can't answer. Headless `csc` clean.
- **Designated floors — Phase 2 of the unified field: MAT stamping + walkable masks**: the arena + pit now *declare* their walkable floor by material and duelists only walk what the paint says. `CombatController.walkableMaterials` / `WalkMask` feeds `CoverField.Walkable`, `EdgeOpen`, `NearestWalkable`, the `DistMap` flood, `BuildRoute`, and `SwingSideScore` (empty mask = physics-only, old behavior preserved). `ArenaWalkableMaterials {102, 106, 111}` = the floor family (sidewalk pad + dark-wood marker pads + painted-metal stand plates); `CityMap3D.AttachCombatStack` seeds it on both duelists when unset — a scene-serialized mask is respected. Assets regenerated (`Tools/gen_cover_arena.py`, `gen_cover_pit.py` → `.stasset`): ground pads `104 Asphalt` → **`102 Sidewalk`** (same MAT id the city terrain stamps); wall lips split to **`108 Dark Trim`** — deliberately outside the family. Previously lips were `106`, same as marker pads — on the pit's h6 null tail (0.30m) or the arena's h7 dip (0.45m = stepUpMax exactly) a `106` lip was a legal staircase over the wall. Walls/curb/stubs/pillars/planters keep 100/101/107 — all outside the domain. Consequence: physically-fitting-but-undesignated surfaces (wall tops, lips, car roofs) can't be stands, corridor cells, or swing destinations — *by authored designation*, not by hoping physics rejects them. `ROUTE_CROSSING_CENTER_WALL_MIDFIGHT` → FIX APPLIED. Headless compile clean.
- **Goal-seeded Dijkstra maps on `CoverField` — Phase 1 of the unified field (megaplan `~/.devin/plans/plan-edd89023dfbda8f7.md`)**: combat routing no longer re-floods per leg. `CoverField.DistMap` = a reverse-Dijkstra product over the declared walkable channel — the cell-scale sibling of `Pathfinder`'s `FlowField`: `dist[]` meters-to-seed + `next[]` hop-toward-seed + `Reaches`/`CostFrom`/`Next`/`ReachedCount`, stamped to `field.BuildStamp` (never read across builds). `BuildDistMap` floods 8-connected with gates IDENTICAL to the old leg flood (`Walkable ∧ EdgeOpen ∧ no corner-cutting`) so "the map reaches it" ≡ "a corridor exists" structurally. Controller holds two: `meMap` (seeded at the feet cell — rebuilt when the field restamps or his cell moves) and `anchorMap` (seeded at the search anchor — geodesic distance from belief, around walls not through them). Consumers now read instead of re-derive: `PickBest`/`PickNear`/`TryRankInZone` take `reach` and skip unreachable cells/members (`PickBest` tallies `unreachable` rejects); the pick's wall-mark fallback is reach-gated too (reject, don't strand); `TryOrbitStep` skips unreachable zones, scores `|anchorMap.CostFrom − buffer| + meMap.CostFrom·0.15` (Euclidean fallback when a map can't serve — e.g., sealed pockets), and the exhaustion census names `unreachable`; `TryRelocateToFightingCover` + `TryNearestCover` gate zones by `ZoneReachable` (reachable ∧ Hidden member — the corridor-dead livelock class dies at pick time); `SwingSideScore` requires the step-out to physically host a body (`Walkable`). `BuildRoute` is now a pure materializer: `map.Reaches` gives the fenced verdict up front (`"map unreachable — flood serves N/M"`, same diag slot), then `ChainToSeed` walks next-hops and the string-pull is unchanged. The per-leg flood, `legPrev`/`legCost` scratch, `routeRingBias`, and `SnapWalkable` are all gone (ring-hugging is a scoring term now, not a flood bias). `failedFaces`' corridor-dead write stays as dormant belt — the map should starve it. Compile clean. Expected behavioral change: unreachable stands are never committed — watch for `unreachable` in orbit-exhaustion logs and zero `no corridor` legs.
- **Final-look rise refusal gets the same peek cascade as every other beat** (playtest — Tan hit `orbit done at 142° → final confirming look → rise refused: can't see over: wall 0.90+0.02 vs eye≤0.76 → abandoning`; user: "the final look should still use the side step peek if rise isn't achievable, just like the other beats"): the `orbitFinalLook` gate in `EnterBeat(Rise)` exited BEFORE the refusal cascade — a doctrine inconsistency, not a verdict. Now: terminal-look refusal → `EnterBeat(Swing)` exactly once (the swing's return lands at a second `Rise` whose contact/no-contact verdict still owns the exit); a burned peek (already swung from this stand) or a swing that can't start (no zone rim) abandons honestly — bounded: at most one peek excursion, then the honest terminal. Non-final-look cascade unchanged.
- **Phase-0 field collapse verified live** (playtest): unchanged duel ran as designed — seek→cover→rise→duck→fire→search/orbit both fighters; startup ~120 FPS vs the previous degraded 40 (per-build re-probing retired → field-lifetime probe cache). Filed `ROUTE_CROSSING_CENTER_WALL_MIDFIGHT`: mid-fight Vinny committed a route through the center arena wall — reachability-after-commit class; expected structural fix = Phase 1 goal-seeded maps gating picks.
- **`Thud_ricochet.stsound` — footstep-based ricochet (hand-authored file)** (`sounds/ricochet/`, user request — "brand new ricochet, call it Thud_ricochet, base it off the footstep"): the footstep preset's two layers verbatim as the strike (`attack 0.40/90` + `noiseTail 0.35/30/lp800`, seed 11 to keep its exact noise flavor) + the fitted whine genome (dive 2100→880 + rise 1250→2150, harmN 2). `masterAttack:0.012` — a 12ms ramp that softens the whine sines' t=0 onset ding while leaving the noise thud intact; `masterRelease 0.45`. Not a catalog member — won't be overwritten by `gen_ricochet_catalog.py`.
- **Stone strikes → the footstep recipe VERBATIM (the literal swap)** (`gen_ricochet_catalog.py`, user direction — "swap the ricochet strike with the footstep" after correcting my earlier footstep-*flavored* misread): the `attack`/`noiseTail` strike pair inside `ricochet_stone_01`–`06` is now the footstep preset's own two layers — `{attack 0.40/90} + {noiseTail 0.35/30/lp800}`, no crack/cutoff/rescale. `stone_01` = literal ×1.0 (pure A/B vs the footstep); `stone_02`–`06` = same two layers amp-scaled ×1.2–1.5 (the "is footstep enough or does a bullet need it louder" comparison set). Footstep preset, metal catalog, `ricochet_dl`, `ricochet_01`, and all whine genome untouched.
- **Stone v3 — zero-resonance strikes (the "metal pans" fix)** (`gen_ricochet_catalog.py`, user verdict — "still sounds like 2 metal pans smacking together"): crack alone couldn't fix it because the problem was spectral, not textural — ANY single-resonance burst (tone band at 1100 OR 400Hz) is a formant = struck resonant object = pan/tom. Stone strikes now use **no bandpass at all**: two broadband noise bands shaped by `cutoff` only — `snap()` (bright fracture, cutoff 4.5–9.5k, decay ~170–200, crack 0.75–0.85) + `thud()` (dark mass body, cutoff 0.9–1.3k, decay 80–95, crack ~0.4). Strike partials also cut to ~0.10–0.16 amp/decay 13–15 (they were ringing pitched overtones AT the onset = the other half of the pan). `stone_06` hybrid keeps its one deliberate resonance (the metal-edge impulse ping — that one is supposed to ping).
- **Attack `crack` — fracture texture, the tom killer** (`audio_studio.html` + `ProceduralAudio.cs`, user verdict — "the very first sound is a Roland tom tom / electric drum"): a smooth exp-decayed bandpassed noise burst IS the 909 tom recipe — the strike needed *fracture*, not a cleaner envelope. New attack field `crack` (0–1): block-rate stochastic gain (~1.1ms grain) inside the first ~35ms — random pops/dips break the smooth decay into a shattering crack. Applies post-filter so the crackle hits the shaped band itself; rng draws only exist when `crack>0` so old recipes render bit-identical. New knob on the attack box (`amp · ring · tone · tone q · cutoff · crack`), per-layer `cN`/`cG` state, mutator jitter. Stone family re-voiced: strikes get `crack` 0.65–0.85 AND the chirp thump → **`body()` — a low noise burst ~400Hz** (the massy weight without the falling-pitch tom oscillator the chirp literally is). Metal catalog + `ricochet_dl` keep their chirp — user-approved there.
- **Stone ricochet family — surface differentiation** (`gen_ricochet_catalog.py` → `ricochet_stone_01`–`06`, user feedback — "strike sounds like a metal plate/gong, but projectiles skip off streets, brick, concrete"): the gong was physics, not tuning — metal rings (high-Q resonator), stone can't sustain resonance (damped broadband crack). Stone voicing of the same glide genome: `tone`→800–1.4k @ `toneQ`≤1.0 (wide band = damped = no ring), `cutoff` 2–4k (sizzle gone), partials →0.15–0.3 amp/decay 10+ (die fast), chirp thump →0.4–0.5 (energy into mass), noiseTail →0.2–0.3 @ 2–4k (chip/spall grit), drive →~2.0. The whine layers survive almost unchanged — bullet tumble is the projectile's physics, not the surface's. Flavors: `stone_01` concrete reference, `02` asphalt thud (tone=0 raw dark noise), `03` brick clack (fired clay — hardest, slight 1.7k ring), `04` long street whine, `05` chip&spall (two noiseTails: 130ms grit burst + low dust-settle — first multi-noiseTail recipe), `06` kerb-edge hybrid (stone body + quiet impulse ping for grazing metal). Runtime pick-by-surface is a call-site choice — no code wired.
- **sounds/ cleanup — ricochet family gets its own folder, SFXWIP deleted** (user request — "cleanup the sound folder and my mess of ricochet sounds"): all `ricochet_*.stsound` (+`.meta`, GUIDs preserved) moved to `Assets/StreamingAssets/sounds/ricochet/`; the analyzer source `ricochet.mp3` moved to `ricochet/reference/` (deleting it would orphan `ricochet_dl`'s regen path). `SFXWIP/` deleted entirely — per user choice that included the `ricochet_07_08` family (hand-tuned 3.6s variant + 12-child TestCatalog + the misnamed duplicate; unregenerable, superseded by the v2 catalog). `ricochet_01`'s internal `name:"ricochet_09"` lie fixed → `"ricochet_01"`. New runtime path: `ProceduralAudio.GetClip("sounds/ricochet/ricochet_XX.stsound")` (no code referenced ricochet paths — only gunshot, unmoved). `gen_ricochet_catalog.py` + `analyze_sfx.py` output dirs retargeted to `sounds/ricochet/`. Studio: bind `sounds/ricochet/` for the ricochet library, `sounds/` for gunshot.
- **💾 save → Save-As dialog (overwrite protection)** (`audio_studio.html`, user request — "save should prompt me … prevent overwriting unless I explicitly do it"): the old handler silently wrote `(recipe.name).stsound` into the bound dir — and since recipe internals can lie about filename (`ricochet_01` holds `name:"ricochet_09"`), a save clobbered the wrong file (that's how `ricochet_dl` got overwritten mid-session). Now `💾 save as…` → `showSaveFilePicker` (suggests the *loaded file's* name, `startIn` = bound sounds dir, `.stsound` type filter) — every save picks name+location, and overwriting an existing file routes through the OS "replace?" prompt = explicit consent. Non-Chromium fallback keeps a `confirm()` gate; cancel (`AbortError`) no longer alerts.
- **Reference-derived ricochet catalog v2 — every variant is a child of the MP3 fit** (`VoxelAssetStudio/gen_ricochet_catalog.py` → `ricochet_02`–`ricochet_13` rewritten): the first catalog was hand-invented Hz tables; this one re-voices the *measured* genome — stacked noise@2928 + chirp-thump attack, dive+counter-RISE harmonics (harmN 2–4 @0.5–0.65), 1.2–1.7k ring partials, faint 6–7k air tail, drive ~1.6, masterRelease = 0.3·dur. Variation axes: register (±40–60%), glide spread, rise-vs-dive prominence, exciter stacks (impulse/noise/chirp), AM only where deliberately stylized (`_11` is the sole vib entry — honest cartoon; `_07` the 4-harmonic howler; `_12` twin detuned dives beating together). `ricochet_dl` stays the fitted reference; `ricochet_01` WIP untouched.
- **Phantom vibrato fix — `fit_vib` now demands periodicity** (`analyze_sfx.py`, user catch — "your vibrato selection doesn't match"): the fitted 3.5Hz/172¢ wobble was a fiction — residual analysis showed one +268¢ tracker outlier + a sustained −100¢ glide-curvature plateau (quadratic fit gains only 0.93→0.947; ~2.5 uneven "cycles" in 0.57s ≠ a LFO). Fix: clip bin-hop outliers at median±4·MAD, then require autocorrelation ≥0.30 at the candidate lag — curvature/noise fails the gate. Re-run: `no vib`, recipe now emits plain glide + harmonics. General lesson: real bullet tumble is aperiodic; a sine LFO can only ever render *clean* warble.
- **Sweep `harmN`/`harmAmp` + `amRate`/`amDepth` — the harmonic glide (the "sounds synth" fix)** (`audio_studio.html` + `ProceduralAudio.cs`, user diagnosis — "synthesized ricochets don't sound good even with the exact reference"): the sweep emitted a lone sine = perceptually a test tone. A real ricochet whine is a fundamental + overtones gliding together + the tumble's loudness pulse. New sweep fields: `harmN`(0–4) extra harmonics at 2f–5f riding the same pitch track with `harmAmp` rolloff — sine→resonant howl; `amRate`/`amDepth` loudness LFO = the AM half of the tumble (vib was FM-only). Analyzer now FITS harmonics too — measured the real ricochet at harms 3@0.59 (confirming the diagnosis); `ricochet_dl` regenerated with fitted harmonics+vib. Catalog's primary whines updated (_02/_03/_07/_09/_13 get harmN 2–3 + AM tumble; _05/_12 subtle). Backward-compatible everywhere.
- **`analyze_sfx.py` — mp3/wav → .stsound reference converter (v2: real fits)** (`VoxelAssetStudio/analyze_sfx.py`, user request — "mp3 to synthesized converter … how close can we convert"): decodes via ffmpeg, fits structure not samples — onset/decay envelope, coherent-run glide tracking (chaotic strike region breaks coherence → the whine segment wins), strike-band + low-thump detection (noise attack + chirp under-layer), ring partials WITH per-partial energy-weighted decays, and v2's new fits: **band-energy amp attribution** (no more hardcoded layer amps), **vibrato from glide residual FFT** (detected a real 3.5Hz/172¢ tumble in the source), **echo from envelope autocorrelation**, **drive from crest factor**. Verified on the downloaded ricochet: 2216→931Hz dive (r²=0.93) + 1379→2394Hz rising counter-whine + 2928Hz strike + 236Hz thump → `ricochet_dl.stsound` in `sounds/` — feed it to the Variant Lab as a catalog base.
- **Attack-layer `src` exciters — noise | impulse | chirp (different-sounding strikes)** (`audio_studio.html` + `ProceduralAudio.cs`, user request — "bare exciter is a hollow coconut … how do we get different sounding strikes"): a bandpassed noise burst IS the coconut recipe (impulse→one resonance). New `src` select: `impulse` = single-sample spike → the resonator rings clean (struck-bar "tock"); `chirp` = pitch-dropping sine over the decay window (`startHz`→`endHz`, ~750→110 = kick-drum thump/punch — the exciter noise cannot make). Stack two attack layers = crack+body (the classic punch). tone/cutoff still shape whatever comes out. C# mirror, conditional chirp knobs, mutator jitter; catalog _02 gains a chirp under-layer (crack+thump), _13 an impulse layer.
- **Attack-layer `cutoff` — independent click brightness** (`audio_studio.html` + `ProceduralAudio.cs`, user request — "i want a cutoff filter here"): 1-pole lowpass chained AFTER the tone bandpass — shaves sizzle/darkens a harsh strike into a thud and softens the resonance's ringing edge; 0 = off (legacy). New knob on the attack box (`amp · ring · tone · tone q · cutoff`), C# mirror, mutator jitter; catalog _08 snap gets 14k, _11 concrete gets 4k (a genuinely dull stone hit).
- **Attack-layer `tone`/`toneQ` — the click gets a resonant band (fixes "clunk")** (`audio_studio.html` + `ProceduralAudio.cs`, user request — "shape the click … more impactful … a tone knob"): the attack was raw white noise × exp decay — broadband DC-to-22k energy reads as muddy clunk + harsh sizzle. `tone` (Hz, 0 = legacy white) runs the click through an RBJ bandpass (constant-0dB peak, center scales with pitch): ~500 = tok/thump, ~2–3k = metallic ka-, 5k+ = tick; `toneQ` widens/narrows the resonance — high Q makes the filter itself ring a pitched edge onto the strike. Per-layer filter state in both engines, rng order unchanged → bit-exact parity, backward-compatible. Two new knobs on the attack layer box; mutator jitters live values. The ricochet catalog attacks are now toned (2.4–3.4k metallic, q 1.2–1.8; _11 concrete thump 1.3k).
- **MASTER attack/release knobs — global mix trims (audio schema v4 continues)** (`audio_studio.html` + `ProceduralAudio.cs`, user request — "master also needs attack and release knobs so i can trim even further"): `masterAttack` = raised-cosine fade-in on the whole buffer (0 = instant strike — the ka- hits full force; small values soften the onset into a swell); `masterRelease` = raised-cosine fade-out over the buffer's last N seconds — shapes how the WHOLE tail dies (whine+echo+noise together), composing with `tailFade` which stays the click guard. Both absent/0 = off = identical v3 renders; mirrored in `SynthFromRecipe`; mutator jitters them when master is unlocked; the ricochet catalog now ships `masterRelease ≈ duration×0.3`.
- **Sweep layer gains `curve` + vibrato (audio schema v4 — ricochet whine controls)** (`audio_studio.html` + `ProceduralAudio.cs`, user request — "more whine / bend the other way"): the sweep ramp was a linear Hz/sec slide — perceptually it slows as it falls, and there was no wobble. New per-layer fields: `curve:"exp"` interpolates `start·(end/start)^k` = equal semitones/sec — a true musical dive AND a rise (`endHz > startHz` gives the ascending-ricochet variation); `vibRate`(Hz)+`vibDepth`(cents) add a vibrato LFO on the sweep frequency = the tumbling-bullet warble (8–15 Hz / 50–150 ¢ reads as ricochet). Backward-compatible: absent fields = "lin"/off so v3 recipes render identically. Sweep knobs widened to 20 Hz–12 kHz + a curve select in the layer box; the mutator jitters live vib params; the `ricochet` preset now demos it (exp · 11 Hz · 90 ¢). `PROCEDURAL_AUDIO.md` schema → v4. **Follow-up (user insight — "there is 2 LAYERS to ricochet")**: sweeps already stacked (per-layer phase in both engines) — the gap was UX: `+ add` used to cycle types by layer count, now a type picker (defaults to `sweep`; new sweeps default to `exp` curve). The `ricochet` preset gains the second whine (1850→520 exp · 14 Hz/120 ¢) under the main dive — two detuning glides = the classic double-tone ricochet. **Showcase catalog — LONG-TAIL pass** (`VoxelAssetStudio/gen_ricochet_catalog.py` → `Assets/StreamingAssets/sounds/ricochet_02…13.stsound`): 12 hand-tuned recipes exercising the new options, re-tailored for long tails per user feedback — durations 1.1–2.2s, layer decays 4–7 (ring-out ≈1.0–1.7s), echo feedback 0.35–0.55 so the whine blooms and dies naturally while the attack stays a sharp "ka-". Dual-sweep ka-pwang reference (_02), octave-down deep pwang (_03), ascending whine (_04), fast shimmer (_05), crossing glides (_06), longest movie whistler (_07), snap-attack/ringing-tail (_08), lin-curve A/B of _02 (_09), extreme 320¢ siren warble (_10), gritty concrete scrape (_11), thin distant mosquito (_12), triple-sweep kitchen sink (_13). `ricochet_01` (user's WIP, internal name `ricochet_09`) untouched — note its internal name ≠ filename, so 💾-saving it in the studio writes `ricochet_09.stsound`.
- **Field-owned walkable truth — Phase 0 of the unified field (megaplan: `~/.devin/plans/plan-edd89023dfbda8f7.md`)** (architecture: "we derive definitions instead of authoring them" → declared channels): walkability was a composite re-derived per consumer — `legNoGround`/`legGroundDone` per-route-build scratch on the controller + `CorridorFree(p,p)` + inline edge gates. Now `CoverField` owns what a cell **is**: `flags[]` (`CellFlags.Grounded|FitOk|FitBad` — tri-state, lazily evaluated once per Build), `floorMat[]` (the floor voxel's material id — `VoxelRayHit.material` rides the same ground cast, free), and a unified lazy `groundY[]` (`floorOf[]` merged away: NaN = unprobed, NoGround = probed-miss — the old `gy==0f` ambiguity that needed `legGroundDone` is gone). New field API: `Walkable(idx, floorMask, bodyH)` = Grounded ∧ 3-point column fits (same 0.06/0.35h/0.6h sampling as the movers' `Free()`) ∧ (empty mask, or `floorMat ∈ mask`); `EdgeOpen(a,b,stepUp)`; `GroundPoint(idx)`; `StandY(idx)`. `CombatController` delegates: `CellWalkable`/`CellGroundPoint` are one-line field calls, both leg HashSets deleted, the flood's edge gate reads `f.EdgeOpen`. `walkableMaterials` int[] added (empty = physics-only — Phase 0 must be behavior-identical; painting the arena floor is Phase 2's gate). `DeriveCandidates` posts use `StandY` so Invalid cells don't dive to the sentinel. RouteFail/`noCorridor` stages untouched.
- **Per-fighter world viz — both duelists' cover fields, rings, beliefs, and orbits** (request: "comprehensive debug visuals covering both fighters and their states — like how cover quality radius works for both"): every Vinny-bound beam set parameterized by tag and drawn for Tan too. Vinny keeps legacy names/palette; Tan's carry `_t` with a dimmed warm palette so overlapping sets stay readable. `PaintSearchViz(cc, ch, opp, tag, dim)`: truth X on each opponent (`pick_aim_t` marks VINNY's feet for Tan's hunt), magenta `last_seen` belief post + beam, orbit spokes (`search_ref/aim`), standoff ring (`search_buf*`), hop beam (`search_pick`), routed corridor (`search_leg*`), destination post (`search_tgt`). `RebuildCoverSpots`/`PaintCoverSpots` now take the target list + tag + palette — Tan's zone posts paint amber beside Vinny's cyan. NEW `PaintRadiusRing`: 24 chords at `sensor.radius` around each fighter — the scan-reach bubble the spots live inside. `PaintFighterBeams`: `cov_dst`/`cov_feet*`/`scr_tgt` per fighter. Also fixed a stale-beam leak: `pick_aim`/`pick_aim2` were never cleared on combat end.
- **Dual combat panels — full readout per duel participant** (request: "need dual hud debug panels to display VINNY and TAN SUIT metrics simultaneously … track their cover choices and orbit search patterns"): `CoverMetricsPanel.BuildText` refactored — the per-fighter block (pose/duck · combat telemetry · beat bar · engagement-flow + SEARCH/ORBIT/FLANK lanes · sensor scan + rejects) is now `AppendSubject(label, ch, isVinny)`, rendered for BOTH Vinny and Tan (the old 4-line tan stub is gone). Possess-only annotations (`POSSESSED`, threat pin, R-reload hint) gate on `isVinny`; the drive ring stays a shared section. Tan's cylinder/RELOAD wheel/beat/orbit/exposure are now visible live — reload mechanism itself was always shared (`StartReload`/`TopUp` in the one CombatController); what was missing was telemetry.
- **Vision wedge beams — per-sensor keys** (playtest: "why only tan suit emitting vision beams if we have parity it should be both"): the scene-space cone (`vs_e0`, `vs_a*`, `vs_center`, `vs_t*`) used FIXED beam names — both sensors' `Redraw` overwrote the same keys → last Update won → only Tan's fan drew. `Beam()` now prefixes the owner name (`{name}_vs_e0`); one-time legacy-name wipe on first Update clears the orphaned shared names. The shader cone (mode-7 `_VisCone`) stays single-channel — `PreferredOwner` = Vinny unless the vision tab deliberately hands it over; the scene wedges are per-sensor and both draw.
- **`PreferredOwner` — the cone can no longer drift to Tan** (playtest: "tan suit still owns vision cone"): the spawn claim covered only the moment it ran — every later null window (an owner's `OnDisable` clears `ConeOwner`, toggle-off, pre-spawn frames) fell back to all-write → last Update wins → Tan's cone. `VisionSensor.PreferredOwner` is the standing default behind explicit claims: `PushConeGlobals` gates on `ConeOwner ?? PreferredOwner` — Vinny always draws unless the vision tab deliberately hands ownership over; toggling off returns to Vinny instead of a race. `OnDisable` clears both when they point at it.
- **Relocate livelock — corridor-dead stands now mark their face failed** (playtest: "they perform perfectly symmetric fighting which i think is a win but then the log showed this as they both stopped at same time" — rise refused → relocating → `no corridor` → hold → rise refused, repeating): the refusal cascade (burned-stand → relocate / else swing side-step → relocate → pin) worked, but `failedFaces` only recorded *rise-solve* failures. A committed stand whose `BuildRoute` corridor died stayed in the relocate pool → the abort reset `CoverPoint = Feet()` → next refusal re-picked the SAME unreachable stand → corridor dead → hold → refuse → ∞. `noCorridor` now adds the destination obstacle's nearest face-mark to `failedFaces` before the abort — the pool shrinks every dead commit until `found=false` → `pinned` ("stand can't fight, no peek edge, no fightable cover") — the honest terminal the cascade was designed to reach. `routeFail` added: the corridor verdict now names its failure stage (`legs-off`/`no field`/`cell-of`/`no walkable cell`/`flood fenced N/4096`) in the log + DUEL row.
- **Route flood drowned in unprobed ground — lazy `groundY` probe** (DUEL-status row read `no corridor`, `dst 0`): `CoverField.groundY` is only probed under stand-band cells (~180 of 4096) — every open cell keeps `Array.Clear`'s 0 = street level, so `CellWalkable`'s 3-sample column landed inside the raised deck slab → `CorridorFree` false → the whole open field read solid → the flood could never cross the 6.4m stand→cover gap → `noCorridor` → instant `HoldPosition` "stand + fire". `CellGroundPoint` now lazily raycasts unprobed cells (same 1.6/3.2 contract as the field's `ProbeFloor`), caches hits back into `groundY` for the scan's life, and marks misses `legNoGround` (ungrounded = unwalkable; `CorridorFree` also rejects NaN endpoints). `legGroundDone` dedupes per build. Also split the abort's `LastResult`: `no corridor to X` vs `cover unreachable`.
- **DUEL — live status fold** (Combat tab, under START DUEL): one row per fighter — `State/Beat · arm · duck→target · dst→cover` + `LastResult` verbatim + scan line (`walls · valid/tested cells · radius · field res`). Turns "why didn't he move" into a screen read instead of a console dig. First catch: the unprobed-ground flood failure above.
- **Tan's cone painted over Vinny's — `ConeOwner` claimed at spawn** (playtest: "tan suit took over the vision cone POV"): the only claim site was `DrawVisionTab` — unopened tab → `ConeOwner = null` → every sensor's `PushConeGlobals` writes and the last `Update` wins (Tan's order landed after Vinny's). Now claimed deterministically when `SpawnedCharacter`'s sensor attaches; the vision-tab `coneOwner` toggle still hands it to whichever sensor is being inspected. Probe/impact marks unaffected — those are the owning sensor's per-sensor viz.
- **START DUEL never sought — stands outside `Sensor.radius`** (playtest: "both seek cover has them standing still and firing at each other, no move to cover"): the arena plates (`cover_stand_a` z≈2.45m / `cover_stand_b` z≈16.65m) sit **6.4m** from their nearest cover rows (z≈8.9/10.3m), but `CoverSensor.radius` had been shrunk `8f → 4f` in the measured-silhouette rework — the `EnterCombat` scan saw zero walls → `PickBest` fails → `TryPickCover` false → the no-cover fallback: `HoldPosition` + armed → standing open firing. Symmetric by construction (mirror-distance stands) so both turreted identically. The bare duel had **never** sought cover — every earlier successful seek rode `TryPickCoverNear(anchor)` via the deep/mid/crouch/wall pick row (radius-independent). `radius = 8f` restores the last working value; filed `PIT_STANDS_OUTSIDE_SENSOR_RADIUS`.
- **Tan parity — `VisionSensor` joins `AttachCombatStack`**: the pit's secondary rig deliberately skipped it ("Vinny-only — player-facing debug sense"), but `ConeCovers()` treats a missing sensor as *no gate* (`vis == null → true`), so Tan fought with **ungated 360° geometric detection** — no posed-eye fan, no head-tracking requirement, contact on raw LOS unocclusion while Vinny needed voxels inside his posed cone. `AttachCombatStack` (shared by Vinny's spawn path — his explicit add stays a no-op) now gives every duel participant the same detection contract: posed-eye-fan + voxel resolve. Everything else audited symmetric: `CharacterRig` (VoxelCharacter + CharacterAnimation + WeaponMount/SW_Model_10), solver/sensor get-or-add, `EnterCombat` + `RefillCylinder` both sides, `holdInCover=false` in Fight mode, `PRIORITY_COMBAT(30)` outranks pedestrian scripts. Debug-viz note: `ConeOwner` still arbitrates the shared _VisCone globals — only one cone *draws*; detection is per-sensor either way.
- **"No corridor" is a verdict, not a ray fallback** (playtest: Vinny grinding a pillar face, `seek` yaw whipping ~190°/frame): a failed `BuildRoute` on a live cover field fell back to a blind straight-ray at `CoverPoint` — slide along the face, bearing whips through the node's XZ line, nudge budget burns, abort, orbit re-picks behind the same wall → the loop. `noCorridor` now rules the leg unreachable immediately and takes the existing abort path (`TryOrbitStep` re-pick while searching, `HoldPosition` otherwise). Off-field/routeLegs-off legs still take the honest ray.
- **Heard-shot stimulus — reports update last-known** (request: "shot reports from the opponent should update last known position"): every `FIRED` now calls `ThreatCharacter`'s `OnShotHeard(Feet())` — a bang is spatially-exact intel: `ThreatPos`/`LastSeenTarget`/`lastConfirmed` snap to the firing position (zones re-paint free) and a live orbit `ArmSearch()` re-anchors on the bang — shots re-base the hunt at the real spot, silence lets the arc complete. `contactSeen`/`LastSeenAt`/`convergeSince` deliberately untouched — a report is intel, not a sighting; arm-hold grace + drop clock keep eye-channel truth. Scoped to the declared threat — a radius broadcast is the crowd-scale version. Symmetric: Vinny's shots teach Tan where Vinny is.
- **Fire-and-maneuver posture consolidation — `ExposureArmed` owns the fold** (playtest: armed character rendered in a deformed floating crouch instead of stand-and-deliver): the duck channel answered "what does the COVER want" (destination `targetDuck`, scout `lreq`, seam req) while the arm verdict already said "engaging" — armed + deep fold = crumple-with-gun-out. Three fold writers now gate on `ExposureArmed` — the same verdict that picks `Aiming`/`AimSweep`/`AimWalk` and gates the trigger: armed → duck eases to **0** (upright firing posture) in seek-transit, scout-on-covered-ground, and HoldPosition; unarmed keeps the context fold (arrives pre-folded). The only legal armed fold stays `riseDuck` (the rise solve's bore-clearance requirement). Pose and posture can no longer disagree — one question, two renderers. Updates `RENDER_FLOATS_ABOVE_GROUNDED_ANCHOR` (selection-layer cause fixed; unarmed-fold render remainder open).
- **Step-up capped at arena curb — `stepUpMax` 0.55→0.45** (playtest: "i can also step up on large walls and boxes — however tall the arena curb is should be max"): decoded `cover_arena.stasset`'s voxel grid — the apron/curb ring tops out at 0.4m (west edge), interior cover walls/boxes rise in ≥0.5m tiers to ~1.6m. `0.45f` clears every curb face and rejects every wall tier, so chained "stairs" climbs onto walls/cars stop. Field is serialized: if either controller's Inspector still shows 0.55 (scene re-saved after the field was added), set it to 0.45 — the code default only applies to un-serialized instances.
- **Possess dead-on-entry — `debugCameraFreedom` aliasing** (playtest: "log says released but im just entering it, no controls"): `TryPossess` set `cityMap.debugCameraFreedom = true` to borrow the free-cam clamps — but the same flag doubles as `CamFree`, the input-ownership verdict. Possession flipped `CamFree` on permanently → the yield early-returned every frame → `Active` + `POSSESSED` HUD with zero input. New `CityMap3D.borrowedFreedomClamps` feeds the four clamp sites (ortho size, pitch clamps ×2, orbit distance) without touching `CamFree` semantics; `TryPossess`/`Release` save/restore it and leave the user's F6 flag alone — toggling F6 mid-possession is now the designed inspect handoff and persists through Release. Filed `POSSESS_DEAD_INPUT_FREEDOM_FLAG_ALIAS`.
- **Possess vs camera input — one ownership rule** (playtest: "camera keys and possess keys are sort of mixed up"): three gates disagreed about who owned input under F6-while-possessed — the WASD fly needed `!PossessController.Active` while possess *yielded* on `CamFree` (WASD dead for BOTH), `HandleMouseCamera` yielded at `Active && !Frozen` (mouse camera dead under F6), and `HandleClick` had no possess gate at all (LMB fired the gun AND picked blocks). The rule is now uniform: **possess holds input iff `Active && !CamFree`; free cam (F6/F8) always returns the camera completely** — WASD fly, pan/zoom/orbit, block picks, and a real visible cursor (it unlocks while the fly owns input, re-locks the moment control returns — the possess yield already keeps look angles synced for a snap-free handoff). Possess mode unchanged: WASD moves, mouse looks, LMB fires, R reloads, Shift sprints, G releases.
- **Step-up — one climb rule everywhere the body checks space** (possess playtest: "walk off arena and can't get back up" — and AI legs couldn't path back in either): the walk gate only ever blocked XZ; gravity's snap is ±5cm. New `stepUpMax` (0.55m, serialized on both controllers): on a blocked move, `TryColumnTop` at the destination must return a top within (feet+0.04, feet+stepUpMax] AND the raised 3-point column must be free → he places at the top, gravity snap holds the new deck. Curbs/aprons/thresholds climb; walls still wall. Same `TryMove` on the direct move and both slide axes, in `PossessController` (also gained the waist sample — its column was still two-point) and `CombatController`. Route layer: `CellWalkable` now samples at the cell's OWN `groundY` (`CellCenter` carried the field's uniform `GroundY` — on a two-level field every upper cell's feet sample sat inside the slab → permanently unwalkable, which is why AI could never path back into the arena); the flood's neighbor edges gate on `|ΔgroundY| ≤ stepUpMax`; `CorridorFree` samples step segments at the upper level (climb happens at the boundary face — the old lerp marched samples through the step's side and rejected legal climbs); string-pull waypoints use the per-cell ground point.
- **Two-point body column → three-point (walk/corridor/stand)** (playtest: first successful orbit flank — and Vinny clipped straight into an open-top car's cabin): `Free`/`CorridorFree`/the `CoverField` stand check all sampled feet+top only (0.06m & 0.6h). A car's door band (~0.2–1.0m) fell *between* them — feet read the air gap under the floor pan, chest cleared the sill → the whole body volume entered the cabin legally. Added a waist/mid sample: `Free` + `CorridorFree` at `0.35h` (≈0.63m — inside every door band); the stand check at `cellTop * 0.5` (scaled to the required eye so deep-crouch stands under legal overhang aren't over-rejected). Routes, seeks, and painted stands now all reject car interiors — and any other thin-shell cavity the band used to hide.
- **Blind aim = AimSweep, resolved aim = Aiming** (user doctrine — "sweep depends on whether impact cubes are painting a target; if not, target is hidden → run the sweep; if marks paint, just IK looking"): the stationary armed pose pick now splits on `contactSeen` (the cached `canSee` verdict — probe∧cone, the same signal the green marks report). `!contactSeen` → `AnimState.AimSweep` (state 10 — the aiming pose + authored shoulder/head sweep; `headYaw += sweepYaw×headFollow` so the **cone physically scans** the last-known sector while the arm's IK holds `AimAnchor()`); `contactSeen` → `AnimState.Aiming` (the stare). Applies to every armed InCover moment — blind rises, blind Engaged hold, and the orbit's final-confirming look. Rises never swept before (FaceCurve→fixed stare); the remembered sweep was the swing dwell's `Looking` pose. `AimSweep` was authored but dead — nothing requested it.
- **Trigger pulls now log their own decision line** (playtest: a round went off during a fire-gate flap and the only record was ballistics): the shot site logs `🥊 FIRED #n → (x,z) (vis n · err n° · Beat/State)` — the `CLEAR` transition that produced the shot can be coalesced into `×N flips`, but the pull itself can no longer pass unlogged. Explains the glass hit at 15:01 — a legit flap-to-CLEAR opportunity shot at a resolved centroid; the 0.3° solver residual clipped a window pane 9.5m short (bore-clear to the point, flight intercepted — honest ballistics, not a gate bug).
- **Orbit termination now earns a final confirming look** (user doctrine: "reach full bounded orbit target → final rise and look to confirm angle of last known is clear → duck and settle → end"): `TryOrbitStep` returning false at a settle seam (arc satisfied ≥90° OR hops exhausted) no longer goes straight to `ExitCombat` — `FinalLookOrExit()` sets `orbitFinalLook` and commits ONE real rise from the terminal stand. Its verdicts own the exit: contact → `Engaged` (the canSee edge's `ResetSearch` clears the flag — a live fight resumes), zero contact → "final look — flank clear of him, abandoning" → `ExitCombat`, rise refused → same exit (he can't confirm what he can't see over — no swing/relocate cascade on a give-up). A stray `ArmSearch` under the flag still funnels to the same seam → second `FinalLookOrExit` sees the flag set → exits — bounded, no re-orbit. The mid-leg abort path (stranded in the open, stale cover context) still exits directly. So the full episode reads: seek (opportunity hot) → contact → park/continue roll → cover rhythm → miss → orbit (transit legs settle+move, recon legs settle→rise→duck) → arc target → **confirming look** → end or re-engage.
- **Debug defaults on**: `VisionSensor.showCone` (FOV cone wedge), `CoverSensor.showImpactMarks` (probe impact cubes), and the COMBAT tab's `showCoverSpots` now default on. Cover spots also **auto-refresh** at 0.75s while shown (was manual-button only — verdicts are threat-relative and went stale mid-fight) and clear their beams when toggled off.
- **Eyes-on-threat head channel — the cone POV can now be AIMED** (user: "the cone is the authoritative truth; probes only work because the cone has a target"): the gap was real — walking legs read `headYaw=0` (eyes welded to body-forward → the destination) and the aim-state head yaw is an *authored constant*, so no channel ever posed the head at a live target; during a cover-seek the fan physically couldn't hold Tan → `FIRE·cone off` forever. New chain: `VoxelAnimator.headYawOverride` (radians, body-relative, NaN=off — replaces the layered state pose + lookScan in the gid-1 eval) ← `VoxelCharacter.HeadYawOverride` ← `CombatController` sets it during threat-armed `SeekingCover` legs to the `AimAnchor()` belief bearing (clamped ±1.35 rad neck range; note the animator's transposed `RotationY` convention — the delta is sign-flipped). Feet run the route, eyes ride the target — the transit opportunity channel can produce real contact now. In-cover beats release it to authored poses. CPU-pose only: the eye anchor/cone/marks get it; the GPU-rendered head doesn't visibly turn yet (wedge = truth).
- **Impact marks are now cone-gated per sample** (user: "probe hit boxes should only be live if the cone is valid"): `VisionSensor.Covers(point)` is the single fan definition (posed eye anchor + range + 55° yaw) — `CombatController.ConeCovers` now delegates to it, and `CoverSensor`'s marks color three-way: **green** = resolved ∧ in-cone (the voxels `canSee` actually counts), **slate** = geometrically clear but outside the posed fan (reachable if he turned — never "seen"), **red** = occluder. The viz can no longer claim "seen" where the gate says "not looking" — a slate-lit Tan with `FIRE·cone off` in the log is the whole story in one glance.
- **Fire-gate refusals now log to console** (🥊 `[Combat] — fire gate: <reason>`): transitions only, with a 5 Hz coalescer that reports rapid flapping as `×N flips` instead of flooding — a `canSee` flicker reads as an alternation pattern, not spam. Each line carries context: `vis` count, cone on/off, aim err/blend, beat/state. A `CLEAR` line marks the tick the gate opened (just before the shot).
- **Fire gate now self-reports its refusal** (`CombatController.FireHold` + flow-strip display): the strip's `EXPOSED` is geometric-only (`visible>0`) while the trigger's `canSee` adds `ConeCovers` — plus three more invisible checks (cadence, `fireSettleSec` hold, `BoreBuried`), so a fully-lit chain could hold FIRE with no visible reason. The gate now names its first refusal inline: `FIRE·stowed | cone off | no voxels | dry | beat closed | cadence | losing lock | settling | bore buried`.
- **Probe/vision debug-viz blink fixed — ownership split** (playtest: rays + impact boxes blinked in setup, smooth in combat): three stacked causes. (1) `CoverSensor.Redraw` ran `ClearViz()` → `ClearVisBeams()` on every field rescan, wiping probe rays/marks between probes — invisible in combat (per-frame probe repainted same-frame), visible at setup's 4 Hz probe. `Redraw` now calls `ClearFieldBeams()` only; probe viz is owned solely by `TryGetVisibleVoxels`'s trim loops. (2) `VisionSensor.Redraw` cleared `vdm_` detect marks that `Evaluate` had just drawn — they never rendered at all. Same split: `Redraw` → `ClearBeams()` only; `Evaluate`'s slot-trim owns mark lifecycle; `ClearViz` (both) remains the disable/consumer-loss full wipe. (3) Removed the HUD's `nextManualProbe` 0.25 s throttle — the idle pit probe now runs per-frame, same live rate combat uses. Filed `PROBE_VIZ_BLINK_REDRAW_WIPE` (FIX APPLIED).
- **Audio Studio Variant Lab — structural mutation + variant catalog** (`VoxelAssetStudio/audio_studio.html`, user request — "a multitude of different synthesized sounds that sound similar but also vary, in a catalog I can load and edit"): the old 🎲 only did `seed++` — same structure, different noise. New **Variant Lab** panel: `count`×`depth` (subtle/medium/wild jitter scale) generates N children of whatever recipe is loaded — each child rewrites the recipe's *content*: fresh seed, partial count ±1 with hz/amp re-roll (18 kHz cap), per-layer amp/ring jitter, noiseTail lowpass shift, sweep endpoint jitter + 20% direction flip, 15% layer drop / 15% half-amp duplicate. `lock master knobs` (default on) freezes duration/gain/drive/envelope/EQ/echo — structure-only mutation keeps the family voice; unlocking it jitters master/echo too. Catalog rows load the child into the full editor (all knobs/layers/JSON live — mutation composes, so a variant can itself become the next base), per-slot ↻ re-rolls from the same base (rolling rng — repeated re-rolls differ), and 💾 save all writes `base_01…base_N.stsound` straight into the bound sounds dir. `PROCEDURAL_AUDIO.md` gains the P5 entry. The Unity side needs nothing: `GetClip("sounds/ricochet_03.stsound")` works today; a per-impact random family pick is a future call-site choice.
- **"Sees but can't shoot" is now a RELOCATE verdict, not a firing position** (playtest livelock — Vinny looped rise→swing→rise→swing at a 0.33m-lip car): two stacked gaps closed. (1) `Rise` refusal always swung first for non-scripted actors and `TryRelocateToFightingCover` was unreachable — now the SECOND refusal at the same stand (`riseRefusedAt` per-stand tracking) relocates to fighting cover before another swing. (2) `Engaged` could stalemate on sustained `canSee ∧ bore buried` — `boreBuriedSince` accumulates, >1.2s relocates with a named log. Both reset per-stand. Filed `SEES_CANT_SHOOT_LIVELOCK` (FIX APPLIED).
- **Detection marks on the vision channel** (`VisionSensor.showDetectMarks`, default on): every registered target that's actually visible in-cone gets an x-ray cube at its point, colored by detection level — pale while accumulating, amber mid, red at full. Exposed on both VISION-tab toggle rows; cleared with `ClearViz`. The combat `SetTarget(ThreatFeet())` feed means the swing-dwell contact check now lights its mark too.
- **`visInterval` default → 0** — the probe runs every frame; marks/canSee track live. Rate-independent after the `dropAfterSec` clock change.
- **Probe drop gate is rate-independent**: `emptyProbesToDrop` (count, coupled to `visInterval`) → `dropAfterSec` (0.5s sustained no-contact clock). `visInterval` is now purely a cost throttle — set `0` for a continuous per-frame probe (`canSee` + impact marks track live) without the Engaged drop twitching at frame rate.
- **X-ray debug channel** (`PathDebugRenderer.SetXrayCube`/`ClearXrayCube` + `InstancedColor.shader` gained a `_ZTest` property, default LEqual): the beam material is depth-tested, so cubes painted at silhouette-sample points were buried inside the target's own voxels — x-ray cubes draw ZTest-Always through geometry. Impact marks and the bore orb now use it (marks inside a body / orb welded inside the hand read correctly).
- **Vision impact marks + bore-state orb (debug viz)**: `CoverSensor.showImpactMarks` paints cube markers per probe sample — green at the target voxel a ray resolved, dim red at the occluder point where a ray died — Tan "lights up" exactly where he's seen, without the ray clutter (per-owner keys `vmk_<name>_i`, HUD toggle beside probe rays; manual-probe preview works outside combat too). `dbgBoreOrb` (CombatController) floats a cube welded to `mount.MuzzleWorld` while the arm presents — green = welded bore path to the aim point clear, red = buried — reusing the same per-frame `BoreBuried` eval as the relocate clock.
- **Rise solve de-layered — bore gate is now vertical lip clearance only**: `SolveRiseAtSpot` no longer rays muzzle→target (that answered "does this shot reach", which is the FIRE gate's job — `BoreBuried` on the real welded muzzle at trigger time already owns it). The solve now only asks "can this cover be fought over": eye clears `wallTop+riseMargin`, then presented muzzle (`eye − sightGap`) clears it too. Kills the false `sees but can't shoot` verdicts where the *target* sat behind the cover (the car blocking the car) — that positional problem now resolves honestly at fire time: stand → aim → welded-ray buried → sustained-burial relocate, producing visible aim-then-move instead of refusing to ever stand. `muzzleFwd` retired; v3 diag is now `bore under lip: muzzle <h> < wall <h>+<m>`.
- **PINNED verdict — the terminal answer for an unfightable stand** (playtest livelock #2 — "Settle → Top up → Settle → RISE flips into DUCK" forever): when rise is refused AND no swing edge exists AND `TryRelocateToFightingCover` finds nothing, every exit has failed — the stand is hide-only for this fight. `pinned` parks him at `Beat.None` (head down, cover held) and re-arms ONE real rhythm cycle every `pinRecheckSec` (4s, tunable) — a real rise is a real probe, so a moved threat wakes it honestly; re-refusal re-pins. Covers the Swing-fail, scripted-fallback, and `Engaged` buried-bore relocate-fail paths; cleared on every commit/exit. `NextAfterSettle` preview shows `None` while pinned. Also fixed `failedFaces` over-marking (added every successively-nearer face — could starve the whole relocation pool; now the single nearest face under the 2m gate).
- **Detection stack formally documented** (user: "a fundamental gap in the design logic"): `PERCEPTION_AWARENESS.md` gains **"The detection → contact → aim → fire stack"** — the four-owner contract (VisionSensor cone = detection; probe∧cone = contact; ArmAimSolver two-mode = aim; the trigger gate = fire) with the invariants (contact requires the cone; aim ≠ fire; converged means held; head yaw is physical coverage). The doc's stale "combat probes bypass the cone" clause — the assumption that produced the blind-fire class of bugs — is marked corrected. `EXPOSURE_ENGAGEMENT.md`'s firing table and loop pseudocode updated to `canSee ∧ held-convergence ∧ cadence ∧ cylinder ∧ bore`.
- **Vision cone supersedes aiming** (playtest: Vinny's opportunity snap fired at Tan while the cone pointed at the car): `canSee` and `emptyProbes` now require `ConeCovers(centroid)` — resolved voxels inside the posed eye fan (`EyeDir` ± `halfAngleDeg`, within `range`). Geometric unocclusion alone is not detection — no more flanking-acquired snaps or shots at someone he never looked at. Couplings: `dbgSearchLook` head-sweep now physically widens mid-leg coverage; `FaceTrack(dest)` means legs see where they're going, not sideways — off-axis threats stay unseen until the corridor turns his head (physically honest, tactically real).
- **Arm channel collapse — one IK, two modes** (user: "everything can collapse into IK"): the arm is now TARGETED-IK (`vis.centroid` on contact, `AimAnchor()` belief while peeking at memory) or AT-READY-IK (forward present on scout legs) — both aim-without-fire; the trigger's `canSee ∧ settle ∧ bore` contract is unchanged and separate.
