A `carriedFields` passthrough now caches all editor-owned fields verbatim on every load path and re-emits on export. `Civilian1.json` fully restored: Model-10 parts + mounts + `sitting` params + left-arm `aiming` + handedness marker. **Rule**: any tool that saves a character file must carry fields it doesn't own.
  - **Cover picking → influence field + V-key shader layers** (`CoverField.cs`, new): the discrete candidate list became a scalar field. **Phase 1 quality** (threat-free): every cover-height wall column splats its stand-spot into a ground-aligned grid + decayed dilation ring — builds every scan even with no threat (passive cover awareness paints always). **Phase 2 threat combine** (cheap, only when threat set): facing filter → ground → free-space → threat→posed-crouch-eye LOS on quality cells → `score = quality×proximity`. `TryPickCover` returns the **field maximum**.
  - **V key now cycles 7 modes**: iteration heat / step channels / miss waste / **CoverQuality · CoverThreat · CoverExposure** / off. The packed field (R=score G=quality B=verdict A=proximity) uploads as `_CoverFieldTex` + params each scan; the `VOXEL_DEBUG_VIEW` variant samples it at `worldHit.xz` — **heat paints the voxel surfaces themselves** (wall faces, car bodies, street), not a floating quad. Cover modes discard misses so sky stays normal; chosen cell flashes white. **All legacy beam markers now default OFF** (ring/footprints/walls/candidates/LOS/ground-quad — kept as COVER-tab toggles for deep debug): the V-key field is the single visual language; remaining beams are only user-initiated markers (PICK THREAT/STAND/BLAST crosses), the vision wedge, and aim/tracer lines.
  - **Mode banner**: bottom-of-screen ticker shows the active V-key mode name (color-coded), what it displays, and its live data (`hidden N/M · pick x,z` for cover modes, `wall cols` for quality) — drawn before the panel-visibility early-out so it persists with the HUD hidden. Off = no banner.
  - `VisionSensor`/`CoverSensor`/`ArmAimSolver`/`CombatController` now attach at `SpawnedCharacter` assignment (`CityMap3D.WaitForCharacterSpawn`) — the FOV wedge + cover scan run from frame one instead of waiting for the VISION/COVER tabs' lazy `AddComponent`. **Audit found a latent crash**: `CombatController.EnterCombat` Get-**only**'d `ArmAimSolver` then called `solver.Arm()` unguarded — ENTER COMBAT before the weapons tab ever opened = NullRef on cover arrival; solver lookup now Get-or-Add (matching its `CoverSensor` handling). Tab lazy adds kept as fallback. **Gotcha #10** documents the pattern: tabs inspect, don't construct. (An earlier attempt put the sensor on `HoodSpawner`'s `Hood_Debug` — wrong character; the tabs inspect `cityMap.SpawnedCharacter`.)
  - Keys tab: Alt+pose latch table added under Character Control.

Cone fill is now a real translucent **wedge mesh** (`Sprites/Default`, transform+tint per-frame, geometry rebuilt only on param change); beams keep rim arc, edge rays, pitched center ray, target rays. Dead `coneRays` field removed.

Fat-line imports removed.

Panel copy now matches the editor's wording.

Applies to every tool's tooltip (place/erase/paint/eyedropper/…).

Additive ids — existing paints untouched. Animator eye anchor now prefers `Eyes` centroid → `Face` → head pivot; Vision block status line names the live anchor (`anchor: eyes — N vox @ …`). `regionDefs` exports carry the new list automatically (`activeClothingRegions()`).
- **Region View + face-anchored cone + ray thickness** (user debugging request — "head may be wrongly painted"): third view mode `region` colors voxels by `regionDefs` colors (unassigned → dim slate) so wardrobe painting is inspectable on the POSED model in any anim state. `regionIdx` Int16 flat-index mirror of `regionMap` (O(1) per-voxel, no string keys per frame) + per-region centroids cached at rebuild. Vision cone upgraded to `LineSegments2`/`LineMaterial` — **Ray Thickness slider** (0.5–10px, real geometry not WebGL-ignored linewidth); `anchorFace` (default ON) pushes the painted `Face` region's centroid through the head FK chain as the eye origin, with an eye marker sphere (magenta = face anchor, cyan = pivot fallback) + a status line in Vision controls reporting face voxel count/centroid — a wrongly-painted face shows up instantly as the cone sprouting from the neck/scalp. Vision params export with `.anim.json`.

New `vision` params section (show / range / halfAngleDeg — exports with `params:` to `.anim.json`; both import paths deep-merge defaults into old files). `updateVisionCone` draws a wireframe fan anchored to the POSED head pivot — position + cumulative rotation pushed through the same `computeGroupRotation` chain the voxels use, so the cone physically sweeps with the Looking state's authored head yaw (the scan-mechanic preview before any game code); yaw fan at eye height + true-direction center ray, rides the Unity-View scene mirror for free. Global "Vision Cone" controls appended under every state's param block. New CoverFinder scans a radius via the XZ broadphase and validates cover points with a real LOS raycast from the threat. ### Fix: orphaned domino members could freeze as floating voxels
- **Bug** (`known_issues/runtime/DOMINO_ORPHAN_FLOATING_VOXEL.md`): when a domino head's voxel was overwritten externally — a later shock-front pass AIRing it, or a crack swap — the stale-drop branch removed only the head's `falling` entry. The member (which owns no queue slot by design) kept its `FallCell` + painted voxel but was never stepped again → single suspended cube, exactly the "one floating voxel" the user spotted.
- **Fix**: on stale-drop of a paired head, the member is adopted — becomes a single `FallCell` with a momentum snapshot + its own queue slot (or removed cleanly if its cell also died).
- **Selftest** `[20] (d) orphan rescue`: destroys a domino head mid-fall → member settles to rubble. **ALL PASS (155,755 checks)**, compile clean.

### Workshop defaults layer — the "in-between" tuning baseline + new defaults

### Workshop defaults layer — the "in-between" tuning baseline + new defaults
- **`VoxelDamageMath.WorkshopRes`**: new per-profile baseline between the live sliders and the authored table — `ResTable` seeds from it each domain reload and `ResetResistanceTable` restores it (previously both pointed at `BaseResistance`). Pending tuning values live visibly in `BuildWorkshop()` pending promotion into the authored table. First pending value: **wood 0.45** on all three profiles (was authored 0.25).
- **`RestoreAuthoredDefaults()`**: escape hatch — wipes workshop + live back to `BaseResistance`. **`PromoteToWorkshop(profile)`**: copies the live table into the workshop baseline (session-scoped; permanent promotion = a code edit).
- **MatRes tab buttons**: `reset → workshop` per profile, `promote live → workshop`, `restore AUTHORED`, with a caption explaining the layer semantics.
- **`pairChance` default 0.5 → 1.0** (user preference after the domino entity work — maximum brick read).
- **MatRes provenance colors**: each class name is colored by where its live value came from — **blue** = authored factory, **orange** = on the workshop baseline (preserved for live testing), **green** = tuned this session and unsaved. Legend line above the sliders.
- **MatRes promote-to-authored**: class names are now flat buttons (single-click selects, `►` marker). `promote '{class}' → AUTHORED` rewrites the `BaseResistance` literal inside `VoxelDamageMath.cs` and calls `AssetDatabase.Refresh()` — a real recompile + domain reload reseeds all three layers (exits play mode; editor-gated via `#if UNITY_EDITOR`). The tuning pipeline is complete end-to-end: slider → green → promote→workshop → orange → promote→AUTHORED → blue forever.
- **First graduation**: user promoted wood → authored 0.45 (verified live: name flipped orange→blue mid-session, no errors); the redundant `BuildWorkshop` wood overrides removed so pending only ever holds non-factory values.
- **`docs/core/LIVE_EDITOR_PIPELINE.md`**: formalized the pattern — three layers (live/workshop/authored), provenance colors, click-select + literal-rewrite promote, the promotability contract for new systems, candidate applications (combat metrics, economy, police, order timing), and the future `LiveEditor.Register` registry idea.
- Verified: csc compile clean; `dda_selftest` **ALL PASS (155,755 checks)**.

### Domino entities — preselected pairs register as ONE physics entity (replaces correlated-velocity pairing)
- **The redesign**: the previous "pairing" was velocity correlation only — two cells hashed the same launch velocity but stayed independent `FallCell`s, so per-index axis-order hashing desynced them mid-fall (why pairs visibly separated) and adjacent same-hash pairs stacked into apparent 4-cell mega-bricks (the "2×1 gluing to 2×1" report). Now `SeedFalling` **preselects** pairs during the detonation sweep: `PairPartner` (renamed from `PairSeed`, returns the partner index) picks exactly one 2-cell piece per tile, and the anchor (always the smaller flat index) registers **one shared `FallCell` instance under both dict indices** — `falling[a]` and `falling[m]` are literally the same object, so `vel`/`acc` are shared by reference. The member owns no queue slot; the head steps the whole piece.
- **`StepFalling` domino path**: per axis, both members' targets are evaluated as a unit — both free → the piece moves together (one move, both cells + dict entries); a falling sibling blocking either → the pair waits; static blocking BOTH → shared momentum absorbed on that axis; static blocking ONE → **the piece fractures**: the stuck member keeps its support and goes single, the free member keeps the shared velocity and flies on. Landing requires both members grounded + slow → both cells deposit as **adjacent rubble — a whole brick in the pile**.
- **Escape**: a member stepping out of bounds escapes alone (flyer with the shared velocity); the survivor continues single with its own material intact.
- **`pairChance = 1.0` is now literally 100%**: the tile roll was `byte/255f >= chance`, which leaked ~1/256 tiles (hash byte exactly 255) — fixed to skip the roll at `chance >= 1`. Verified: uniform all-doomed brick grid at 1.0 pairs **256/256 cells = 128 dominoes**.
- **Flyer phase unchanged** (velocity-glued pairs — flyers have no entities; identical launch keeps them adjacent through the transport phase).
- **Stats**: `lastBlastPairs` (domino entities) + `lastBlastPairCells` (cells launched with a partner — domino members + glued flyers) feed a new MatRes last-blast line: `dominoes N · paired cells M`.
- **`dda_selftest` [20]**: (a) domino falls + lands as two ADJACENT rubble cells (glue holds); (b) asymmetric static support fractures the piece — stuck head rests on the block, member falls to the floor; (c) two adjacent dominoes settle as 4 cells — no mega-piece absorption. `[19]` rewritten for `PairPartner` (mutual partner, 1-cell adjacency, in-tile, exact pair-off, 100% coverage report). **ALL PASS (155,755 checks)**.

### Material-resistance tuning tab + debris radial toggle + demolition defaults
- **`flyerRadialVelocity` default off**: blast flyers now spawn at rest (deterministic jitter only) and drop under gravity — the collapse-in-place read the user preferred over radial spray. The Range-tab checkbox "debris radial launch velocity" flips it back on for A/B.
- **`VoxelDamageMath` resistance refactor**: the `MaterialResistance` switch is now a class-indexed table — `ResClass` enum (glass/soft/wood/stucco/brick/stone/metal/veh.metal/other), `BaseResistance[]` authored defaults, live `Resistance[]` clone + `GlobalResistScale`. The same lookup feeds the per-cell kill roll AND the Beer–Lambert occlusion march, so tuning affects shielding too. `ResetResistanceTable()` restores defaults.
- **New HUD "MatRes" tab** (always available, between Range and Keys): global ×scale slider (0–2), nine per-class sliders whose labels carry live kill probability @center / @½radius at the current power dial (e.g. `brick 100%/40%`), reset button, and a last-blast breakdown — crater cells removed per class + detached count + flyers emitted.
- **Blast stats**: `VoxelDamageSystem.lastBlastRemoved[ResClass]` + `lastBlastDetached` + `LastFlyEmitted`, tallied in both `Detonate` and the `DetonateEvent` plan path (plan's `writeMat` is aligned with `writeIdx`; `detachedStart` splits crater vs islands). Cleared by `ResetAllDamage`.
- **Voxel inspector** (`PICK VOXEL`) now appends the cell's resistance class and live effective resistance.
- **Defaults**: `blastRadius` 6 m (was already), `blastPower` 2 → **4** (slider ceiling raised 4 → 8), `frontPasses` 16 → **6**, HUD `windowW` 380 → **560**.
- Verified: csc compile clean; `dda_selftest` **ALL PASS (155,737 checks)**.

### Unified debris architecture — every doomed cell is a voxel, blast through landing (replaces bundled shards)
- **The pivot**: the old shard system (one capped cube per ~few-thousand-cell bucket that poured instant `DepositHeap` pyramids on landing) is **gone**. Blast-removed cells now spawn as **individual flying voxels** at their own world positions — the debris swarm IS the blast cavity's cells, each keeping its own material. "The shard is now only the transport phase of a cell between grids."
- **`ShardPhysics.StepFlyer`** (Unity-free): gravity+drag integrate `vel`; a meter accumulator spends whole `FlyStepLen` (0.2 m) strides, largest-backlog axis first — bounded by `FlyMaxSteps` so a point query is exact where a swept ray would be wasted (the physics-sweep mitigation for ~8k-cell swarms). Solid samples absorb that axis (never tunnels, even at 200 m/s); `Open` hands back `volumeId`+`cellIdx` for handoff.
- **`SolidVolumeSet.SamplePoint`**: broadphase point containment — one 32 m grid cell → few volume candidates → AABB reject → cell lookup. Overlapping volumes: a solid answer beats an open one (debris may not move into material).
- **`VoxelCollisionWorld.SamplePoint`**: `PointKind` (Free / TerrainSolid / VolumeOpen / VolumeSolid) — volumes win over terrain; `FlyQuery` adapter feeds `StepFlyer`.
- **Cross-volume handoff** (`TryEnterVolume`): a flyer stepping into a volume's AIR cell calls `EnsureDamageState` — **a pristine neighboring business flips into damaged state on first arrival** — then the cell resumes as a falling cell in THAT grid with remaining velocity remapped to the target's cell axes + its own voxel written immediately. Debris literally enters a neighbor through a window and rains onto the lobby floor.
- **Boundary escape → flyer**: `EscapeToFlyer` (was `EscapeToShard`) converts an escaping falling cell to a world-space voxel with cell→world velocity; a 0.35 s re-entry cooldown on the source volume stops doorway ping-pong.
- **Emission**: `EmitFlyersRadial` (blast + every shock-front pass) launches cells radially with deterministic per-cell hash jitter; `EmitFlyersShot` (glass puncture) sends them along the shot direction. Blasts exceeding `maxFlyingCells` (8192) **stride-sample by cell hash** — uniform coverage over the whole cavity, deterministic, no truncation at a list boundary.
- **Rendering**: `flyingDraw` (plain `List<VoxelParticle>`) is cleared+refilled per frame; `PathDebugRenderer` batches it into the same quantized-color instanced-cube pipeline as customCubes — **no per-entity string keys**. `MaxInstances` 2048 → 8192 for swarm-size color buckets.
- **Landing policy**: open cell → falling → rubble → slump (unchanged); solid surface → absorbed, fades at `tEnd`; terrain → absorbed then fades (terrain isn't writable); `rubbleDeposit=false` → landed cells vanish.
- **Removed entirely**: `Shard` struct, `shards`, `UpdateShards`, `PushShard`, `TryDeposit`, `SettleShard`, `Linger`, `EmitShards`×2, `EmitPlanShards`, `EmitClusterRange`, `EmitMatSummary`, `RandRot`/`RandAng`, `planBusyUntil`, `planAirCells`, all shard-budget consts (`RubblePerFrame`, `RubbleCellsPerFrame`, `RenderCapCells`, `FrontShardShare`), `rubblePourPerSec`, `rubbleMaxCells`, `shardBounce`, `shardRestSec`, `maxShards`, `debrisClusterM`, `clusterBuckets`. `ShardPhysics.DepositHeap`/`BuildBuckets`/`Step` remain for selftest coverage only.
- **HUD**: "debris cluster m" slider → "cell impulse"; collision toggle label updated (absorb, not bounce).
- **`dda_selftest` [18]**: SamplePoint solid/open/free/second-volume; free flight integrates; entry → `EnteredOpen` payload; 200 m/s absorb (no tunneling); end-to-end handoff → lands as rubble with own material; cross-volume entry into a neighbor. **ALL PASS (155,737 checks)**.
- **Cost model**: ~8k flyers × ~2–6 point queries/frame ≈ 2–5 ms during swarm flight (~1–2 s) vs ~0.1 ms for the old 192-shard sweeps — the price of per-cell fidelity; `maxFlyingCells` bounds it. `EnsureDamageState` clones a pristine target's full array on first arrival (one-time few-ms hitch per newly-struck neighbor).



### Cellular collapse — detached islands rain as in-grid ballistic cells (replaces island shards)
- **Design pivot**: detached islands no longer get AIR'd + packaged into ~58 capped-cube shards that pour into instant pyramids. Every detached cell is marked `falling` **where it stands** — the voxel never leaves the grid, never unpacks; the animation IS the physics. Frame 0 post-blast shows the structure intact minus the crater; then it rains.
- **`ShardPhysics.StepFalling`** (Unity-free, selftest-covered): each falling cell carries `vel`+`acc` in cell units + its packed `vox` (staleness guard — re-blasted or poured-over cells drop out). Per tick: gravity + drag integrate vel; per-axis accumulators spend whole cells on single-step moves into OPEN neighbors (≤1 step/axis/tick — real blast speeds never exceed ~1 voxel/frame at 120 fps, so no tunneling cases exist); axis order rotates by index hash. Static-solid targets absorb that axis's momentum; falling siblings block (the train effect — stacked cells descend as a unit). A cell that can't move, has static support below, and is under `cellLandSpeed` converts to rubble → `slumpQueue` → the existing angle-of-repose pass owns it from there.
- **Impulse seeding** (`SeedFalling`): radial from blast center, `cellImpulse` m/s at the rim falling off to zero at 2× radius, per-cell hash jitter — rim cells are thrown, distant islands simply drop. Yaw-rotated volumes handled (world↔cell space conversion).
- **Boundary escape**: a step outside the volume AABB (lateral or through the roof) emits a `FallEscape` (idx+vel+vox) — `EscapeToShard` converts it to a real 1-cell shard at the world position with the carried velocity — the one legitimate way debris leaves the grid (a cell flung out a window lands on the street). Stepping below the floor row is NOT an escape — that's the ground.
- **`FindDetached` gains `treatAsAir` predicate**: a second detonation mid-collapse no longer lets falling cells anchor the cells above them — they're invisible to connectivity, so a blast during the rain cascades correctly.
- **Queues**: `fallQueue` = iteration slots (moved cells keep their slot; tombstoned on land/escape, compacted when dominant) — allocation-free steady state; `falling` dict is the membership+velocity store.
- **Per-frame flow**: `UpdateShards` → `PumpFallingCells` (budget `FallMovesPerFrame`=24576 moves across volumes) → `PumpRubbleSlump` (landed cells relax same frame) → `FlushDamageUploads` (one GPU upload per volume — falling-cell moves ride the same batch).
- **Tunables**: `cellImpulse` (7 m/s), `cellImpulseJitter` (2.2), `cellFallDrag` (1.6/s), `cellLandSpeed` (0.6 cells/s) — Inspector, alongside the shard params.
- **Removed**: `EmitDebris` + `IslandShardShare` + the `radial` flag on `EmitClusterRange` (island path deleted — blast-front shards unchanged; shard budget leaves headroom for escapes).
- **`dda_selftest` [17]**: column rain conserves mass and stacks on the floor; lateral impulse lands offset; boundary escape reports + clears; re-blasted falling cell drops out (stale guard); two-cell train descends as a unit. ALL PASS (155,728 checks).
- **Status**: compile clean, selftest green; awaiting in-editor tenement detonation — expected: building stands minus crater for a beat → unsupported mass rains in place with blast-proportional scatter → settles into footprint rubble (no instant pyramids, no pour phase).

### Debris animation pacing — turn-cap lift + per-volume upload batching + raycast broadphase
- **Symptom**: after a tenement detonation the rubble animation ran slow for the first half of settling, then sped up linearly to real-time — a scheduler artifact, not a render cost (render scale can't touch it; the terms are CPU-side).
- **Root cause 1 — `RubblePerFrame = 32`**: only 32 resting shards poured per frame, so a ~100-190-shard swarm turn-took ~3-6 frames per pour; survivors got turns more often as the queue drained → the observed linear speedup. Now `192` (= `maxShards`): every waiting shard pours every frame; `RubbleCellsPerFrame` (65536) is the sole governor.
- **Root cause 2 — N GPU uploads/frame**: each `TryDeposit` and embedded-rescue pour called `UploadVoxelEdits` immediately — up to 32+ separate uploads per frame, often into the *same* volume (each re-walking dirty spans + occupancy recompute). All per-frame writes now append into `DamageState.uploadDirty`; `FlushDamageUploads()` runs one `UploadVoxelEdits` per dirty volume per frame after `UpdateShards`/`PumpRubbleSlump`. Blast/puncture/plan-span one-shot uploads stay direct (they must land visibly that event).
- **Root cause 3 — flat O(all-volumes) raycasts**: `SolidVolumeSet.Raycast` scanned every registered volume per call — shard sweeps, support re-probes (now per-shard per-frame with the cap lifted), picks and projectile shots all paid ~3.7 µs/volume-scan. Replaced with a **32 m XZ cell grid broadphase**: volumes insert into every overlapped cell (rebuilt lazily on Register/Unregister/Clear), `Raycast` 2D-DDAs the segment through cells in ray order, dedupes multi-cell volumes via a `seen` stamp, and early-exits at the first cell beyond the best hit. `TestVolume` keeps the segment-AABB → slab → voxel-DDA funnel unchanged.
- **Measured** (`dda_selftest` [13], 800-volume fuzz): **0.43 µs/ray** → ~192 shard sweeps ≈ **0.08 ms/frame** (was ~0.7 ms) — ~9× per-ray; plus the eliminated per-shard uploads and the restored pour rate.
- **Selftest**: section [4] gains 8 broadphase checks — cross-cell nearest-wins, far-hit across empty cells, perpendicular miss, maxDistance across cells, negative coords, diagonal boundary walk, vertical column hit/miss. ALL PASS (155,717 checks).
- **Status**: compile clean; awaiting in-editor tenement detonation — expect uniform pour pacing (no slow-start/linear-speedup), shallower settle-phase CPU dip.

### Perf audit — per-frame scene scans + IMGUI event floods (Range-tab follow-ups)
- **Trigger**: Range tab open dropped 120→40 FPS (IMGUI flood — fixed prior); blast pick armed dropped again (`PathDebugRenderer` scans). Audited all `FindFirstObjectByType` / `FindObjectsByType` / `Camera.main` / `OnGUI` sites for the same two classes.
- **`PathDebugRenderer`**: cached `cachedBridge` / `cachedChunkManager` — `Update()` + `RenderBeamsIntoCamera()` each ran a full scene scan per frame whenever any beam/cube/path was active (i.e. exactly when a blast pick is armed).
- **`DebugHUDManager` clothing timer**: `RefreshClothingSystems` ran every 1s unconditionally — 2× `FindObjectsByType` (array allocs) + 3× `FindFirstObjectByType` = a periodic frame spike with the HUD closed. Now gated on `visible`.
- **`DebugHUDManager` absent-singleton retries**: `RefreshReferences` throttled at 0.5s still rescanned 4 always-absent test rigs 8×/sec while the HUD was open. It now returns completeness — resolved-all retries at 1s (free null-checks), incomplete at 3s.
- **`CityMap3D` EventPlayer**: cached via `GetEventPlayer()` — was `FindFirstObjectByType` per-frame during WASD move and *per-MouseDrag-event* during pan in execution mode (input-rate × scene-scan, the worst combination).
- **IMGUI event filters**: `CityMap3D.OnGUI` (ortho perf HUD) and `FollowCamera.OnGUI` (camera debug HUD) now skip `MouseMove`/`MouseDrag` — both are label-only, nothing consumes drag/move; same class as the DebugHUDManager fix.
- **Checked + clean**: `Camera.main` sites (click/fallback only), `ProjectileSystem`/`VoxelDamageSystem` lazy-cached fields (resolve once), `GameUIController`/`TickSimulation` cached lookups, all spawner init-time scans (bounded), `StressTestSpawner`/`StressTestDiagnostics` TickHUD lookups (6×/sec + lazy-cached, dev-tools only).
- **Custom-cube batching** (`PathDebugRenderer.RenderBeamsInternal`): debris shards each drew `DrawMeshInstanced` + MPB per frame — a live blast swarm (~hundreds of `gshard_` cubes, which persist while their rubble mass pours) = hundreds of draw calls/frame. Now grouped by quantized color (16 steps/channel) into shared instanced draws — the shard alpha fade lands shards in the same ~dozen buckets.
- **Profiler instrumentation**: `Profiler.BeginSample` markers at `HUD.DrawRangeTab`, `PathDebug.RenderBeams`, `VDS.UpdateShards`, `VDS.PumpRubbleSlump` — the pick-armed FPS drop's remaining discriminator couldn't be attributed statically (widgets ~2ms), so the Profiler hierarchy will name it.
- **Status**: compile clean; awaiting in-editor retest (pick-armed FPS, HUD-open idle, profiler read on the drop).

### Macro-cell occupancy skip — two-level DDA (raymarch acceleration)

### Macro-cell occupancy skip — two-level DDA (raymarch acceleration)
- **Motivation**: bombed-out buildings render slower up close (81 FPS inside the demolished tenement vs 120 pulled back, CPU ~0.55 ms) — the flat DDA marches hundreds of empty interior cells per pixel. Sector baking can't help: the air is *inside* the marched bounds.
- **Occupancy bitmask**: one bit per 4×4×4 macro cell, set iff any voxel inside is solid. Built eagerly at chunk creation + `RegisterSector`, recomputed for dirty macros in `UploadVoxelEdits` (≤64 voxel reads per touched macro, ~free on the dirty-span path), lazily on first upload for externally-registered volumes, fully rebuilt in `RefreshChunkVoxels`.
- **Shader** (`VoxelProxyRaymarch.shader`): primary AND shadow marches test the macro bit before sampling; an empty macro jumps to the cell past its exit plane — `toEdge`/`tExit`/`exitT` computed exactly, then all voxel boundaries ≤ exitT are drained per-axis (`tMax.a += tDelta.a` accumulation — fp-identical to sequential `AdvanceDDA`, ties included). `normal`/`currentT` resynced via argmax-of-lastT so a hit on the landing cell shades correctly. Up to 16 chained skips/iteration.
- **Sectors**: merged occ buffer + `_BuildingOcc[i]` word offsets (parallel to `_BuildingMeta`), delivered to the fragment shader via `nointerpolation uint` varying — occ offsets are separate from voxel `bufferOffset` (placements aren't a contiguous macro grid).
- **Opt-in**: `_OccEnabled` per draw — character/prop/animated group paths bind 0 (replaces the shelved design's brick dilation); legacy `RenderComputeChunks` keeps flat DDA.
- **Verification**: `dda_selftest` [16] ports the skip math verbatim — 7,200 equivalence checks vs flat DDA across 6 fills × 400 rays (axis/diagonal/tie-heavy): identical first-hit cell, sampled ⊆ visited, never more samples. ALL PASS (155,709 checks). Revives + supersedes `PERFORMANCE_PATTERNS.md` #13; full design in `RAYMARCH_TRAVERSAL_OPTIMIZATION.md` Phase 4.

### Debris pour-over-time + material blending (settling rework)
- **`DepositHeap` is now incremental**: signature gains `mat2, mix2, maxWrite`. It writes up to `maxWrite` cells per call in deterministic ring order (center-out square layers, −1 half-extent every 2 layers), so a pour resumes the same mound without rewrites; written cells are solid → next tick skips them.
- **Pour**: a rested shard keeps rendering while it drains `rubblePourPerSec` cells/frame (Inspector, default 8000/s); its cube shrinks with ∛remaining-mass (`mass0`/`size0` snapshot in `Emit`) — the chunk visibly melts into the pile. A shard mid-pour outlives its expiry (`willClear`) until mass is spent; fully-poured shards remove themselves.
- **Top-2 material blending**: `DebrisBucket` now records exact dominant/runner-up counts (`majCells`/`othCells`/`mat2`); shards carry `mat2`+`mix2`; `DepositHeap` picks per cell via deterministic index hash weighted by the share — a 60/40 brick/asphalt bucket writes a mottled 60/40 mound (no more majority-vote flattening).
- **Ragged rims**: ~1/8 of each layer's outermost-ring cells are skipped by index hash — keeps the square stepped silhouette, breaks the perfect symmetry.
- **Form-fit deposit**: each cell `DepositHeap` writes drops down its column to the lowest open cell — a mound over a blast crater fills the hole first, then stacks, instead of building a flat base over the cavity. Material/rag hashes recompute on the final cell index.
- **Angle-of-repose slump** (`ShardPhysics.Slump` + `PumpRubbleSlump` per frame, 4096 moves/frame): an exposed rubble cell slides one step down into an open lateral-down neighbor — 45° max slope, hash-rotated direction, deterministic. Only cells `DepositHeap` wrote can move (per-volume `rubble` HashSet + `slumpQueue` on DamageState — structural voxels never slide; moved cells keep their material; re-blasted cells drop out). Vacancies re-trigger neighbors, so piles relax into slopes, ledge rubble spills over edges, and side-blast piles cascade out of the cavity. Budgeted → the settle is visibly animated over frames. Plus an **unsupported-cell fall rule** (checked before the buried test): cells tucked under overhangs drip down instead of freezing into stalactites, and rubble that loses support later crumbles. **Buried-check removed**: the air-above requirement for slides was freezing floor-to-ceiling pillars in a perfect fixed point — the open lateral-down target test is sufficient to lock interior cells, so now pillar sides shed top-to-bottom. `dda_selftest` [15]: column relaxes to a no-slides-left fixed point with mass conserved + supported, ledge cascade off-footprint, hanging-cell drip, determinism, structural immutability.
- **Accounting**: `🧨 debris` log gains a top-3 per-material breakdown (`matId×cells`) — proves whether asphalt piles came from real pad removal; `🧱 rubble settling` shows blend as `mat/mat2@share`. Per-frame cell budget raised to 65536 (a mass-conserving batch can't hitch the window-close frame).
- `dda_selftest` [15] extended: exact bucket share (22/8), pour budget+resume (50+50, no double-writes), ~40% blend spread, ragged-rim gaps. ALL PASS (148,497 checks).
- Fix note: `VoxelDamageSystem.cs` was rebuilt from `control_out` + patch scripts after a patch-script encode failure truncated it mid-write (cp1252 default); all patches now write UTF-8 bytes.

### Free-fire voxel picks (Range tab, above the range-block gate — usable in planning)
- `PICK STAND` / `PICK AIM` / `PICK BLAST` buttons arm a one-shot voxel pick: the next map click raycasts `VoxelCollisionWorld` instead of firing `OnBlockClicked` (`CityMap3D.voxelPickArmed` + `OnVoxelPicked`; miss keeps the pick armed, button re-click cancels).
- STAND teleports the character via `EventPlayer.DebugSnapTo` onto the hit surface (faces the aim point) + green marker beam; AIM overrides the fire target — overrides `tgtWorld` for IK AIM / spread cone / FIRE, re-arms a live solver — + orange cross marker; BLAST stores the picked surface point + normal + volume id for the demolition bench + red cross marker. `clear picks` wipes all.

### Demolition bench (Range tab)
- `VoxelDamageMath.Blast` (Unity-free, selftest-covered): spherical removal — every non-air cell within `radius` rolls `power·(1−dist/radius)·(1−MaterialResistance)`; surviving glass → cracked, non-glass survivors untouched; returns aligned `removedIdx`/`removedMats` lists (shard coloring needs the pre-blast material).
- `MaterialResistance(mat)`: originally glass 0.05 / metal 111 → 0.9 / catch-all 0.5 — now tiered by real palette IDs (see *Tuning, staging & debris physics* below). Bullet puncture stays glass-only; blast removes anything.
- `VoxelDamageSystem.Detonate(volumeId, worldPoint, radiusM, power)`: same COW + dirty-span pipeline as bullet puncture — works on chunks AND baked sectors. Radial shards (velocity = cell − blastCenter, faster, longer-lived) with **per-cell palette color** so debris reads as building parts. Seeded by `blastCounter` — deterministic per sequence.
- HUD: PICK BLAST → `depth into wall`/`radius`/`power` sliders → `💥 DETONATE` — center = picked point − normal×depth (inside-out by construction). Terrain picks rejected (no volume id).
- `PICK BLDG` mode: click any building → blast center snaps to the volume's geometric center (`VoxelDamageSystem.TryGetVolumeCenter` = corner + half-extent; yaw pivots about center). Panel shows `· center`, depth slider disables, orange extent beams (`blast_rx/ry/rz`) preview the radius live at the computed center.
- `RESET DAMAGE` moved above the range-block gate — planning-phase blasts are resettable. Blast log emoji + radius max widened to 15m, later 30m (tenement is ~19×12×19m).

### Connectivity collapse (demolition bench)
- `VoxelDamageMath.FindDetached` (Unity-free, selftest-covered): BFS-flood from every solid cell in the `y==0` anchor layer; structural cells anchor ALL solid neighbors, glass anchors only glass (a pane hangs off its frame, a brick can't rest on a pane). Unreached solids group into 6-connected islands → `detachedIdx`/`detMats`/`islandEnds`.
- `Detonate` runs connectivity between `Blast` and the upload — crater + collapse land in ONE dirty batch/one GPU write. Islands → AIR, then `EmitDebris`: cells spatial-hash into ~`debrisClusterM` buckets → one falling shard per bucket, Boyer-Moore majority-material tint, beam width ∝ ∛count — wall sections read as falling chunks, not confetti.
- HUD: `drop unsupported (collapse)` toggle + `debris cluster m` slider (0.3–3m). Bullet punctures never detach — blast-only by construction.
- `dda_selftest` [9]: pillar severed by hand → cap detaches; bricks on glass fall (glass can't anchor structure); glass column chained to slab survives — 35 cells, 3 islands, ALL PASS (148,326 checks).

### Occlusion-aware blast
- `Blast` is now two-phase: all decisions read the PRE-blast grid, writes apply after — a wall removed this blast still shields what was behind it (blast 1 weakens, blast 2 punches through). `occlusion` param (default on) keeps a raw-sphere path for A/B.
- `MarchTransmit` — Amanatides–Woo march from blast center to each would-be removal; `transmit *= (1 − resistance)` per solid cell crossed. Open air free, glass ~0.95, brick ~0.5/cell, metal ~0.1. Early-out at 2%; only cells passing the base roll pay for the march.
- `VoxelDamageSystem.occlusionAware` field → HUD `occlusion (walls shield)` toggle.
- `dda_selftest` [10]: same patch + same seeds, only the wall varies — open 9 · brick1 4 · brick3 1 · glass 8 removed. ALL PASS (148,330 checks).

### Shock-front detonation (sim → playback hybrid)
- `VoxelDamageMath.BlastPlan` + `SimulateBlast` (pure C#): K expanding-radius passes (`radius·(i+1)/K`, seed `baseSeed+i`) on a scratch clone — interior cells re-roll every front (near-certain kill), fringe cells see the front once; occlusion frees as inner structure dies → the wave accelerates through cleared space. Writes recorded per wavefront: `writeIdx`/`writeVal`/`writeMat` + `passEnds`; connectivity islands append as the last front (`detachedStart`, `islandEnds`).
- `DetonateEvent` → sim on clone → `PlayPlan` coroutine: **the flash** — all blast writes land in ONE upload at t=0 (building instantly scarred); **the front** — debris shards stream outward per wavefront over `frontSec` (expanding motion lives in the particles + amortizes shard budget); **the settle** — unsupported islands drop a beat later. The plan IS the delta — replaying writes reproduces the sim end state exactly (selftest-verified).
- `EmitDebris` gained `idxBase` (plan's detached span doesn't start at 0); `EmitPlanShards` emits AIR writes per front tinted by original material; `WorldToCell` extracted (shared by Detonate/DetonateEvent).
- HUD: `shock front (sim → playback)` toggle + `front passes` (1–12, later 1–24) + `front sec` (0.1–1.5) — DETONATE routes instant vs. event.
- `dda_selftest` [11]: plan replay reproduces sim end state exactly · 4 fronts remove 15 vs single blast's 6 (compounding) · deterministic per seed · first front confined to its 1.75-cell radius. ALL PASS (148,338 checks).
- **Stale-beam fix**: budget eviction (`shards.RemoveAt(0)` at `maxShards`) dropped live shards without clearing their `gshard_N` beams → frozen debris field accumulated per detonation (the "hanging particles" + 13 FPS). `PushShard` now clears the evicted beam (expiry already did). Emission also stride-samples: `stride = airCount/maxShards` per span — a 15m blast no longer instantiates-then-evicts ~100K candidate shards per front.
- `dda_selftest` [8]: synthetic 20³ brick shell + glass band + metal core — 266 cells removed (55 glass / 206 brick / 5 metal at point-blank), all within radius, removal rates ordered by resistance. ALL PASS (148,319 checks).

### Tuning, staging & debris physics (after commit 3e10298)
- **One-click defaults**: the leveling recipe is now the loadout — `shockFront` ON, `frontPasses` 12, `frontSec` 0.5, HUD radius 20m / power 2.5; retuned after glass-monolith calibration to radius 6m / power 2.0 / 16 passes (the path-length fix roughly doubled effective yield — r6/p2/16 is the current loadout). Slider caps widened: radius ≤30m, power ≤4.0 (power >1 is the punch-through knob — `p·transmit` can exceed 1), passes ≤24.
- **Double-crash / double-debris fix**: `ApplyPlanSpan` played a crash clip AND emitted shards on every call, and `PlayPlan` calls it twice (flash + settle) → two crashes and ~2× debris per detonation. It is now writes + `UploadVoxelEdits` only; `PlayPlan` owns the staging — one crash (sized by `totalRemoved`) at the flash, per-wavefront `EmitPlanShards`, silent settle. (An earlier "dup removed" patch targeted a `DamageAudio` call that doesn't exist in this file and silently matched nothing.)
- **Debris physics v2**: shards are tumbling instanced cubes instead of velocity-stretched beams — `Shard` gained `rot`/`angVel`/`size`/`resting`; `PathDebugRenderer.SetCustomCube/ClearCustomCube` (same unit-cube instanced draw). Ground-plane bounce at `shardGroundY` (restitution `shardBounce` 0.35, lateral ×0.6, spin ×0.55); below 0.4 m/s a shard rests for `shardRestSec` (1.1s) then fades. Sizes: 0.5·vs bullet spall / 0.75·vs blast cells / ∛count·vs·0.9 island chunks. Flat plane only — no voxel collision yet.
- **Tiered `MaterialResistance`** (feeds both the kill roll and the occlusion march): glass 0.05 · neon/lamp/flesh/fabric/hair/water 0.10 · wood/tar/terracotta/painted-wood/tire 0.25 · stucco 0.35 · brick/cobble 0.50 · stone/concrete/asphalt 0.60 · tar 0.50 (bumped from 0.25 — roof was caving like a meteor hit instead of holding the inside-out blast shape) · iron/aged metal/chrome 0.80 · painted metal/vehicle body 0.90 · unlisted 0.50. `dda_selftest` [10] now uses real brick (100) cells and adds a wood-wall row: open 9 · brick1 4 · brick3 1 · wood 7 · glass 8. ALL PASS (148,439 checks).
- **Voxel Inspector**: new `PICK VOXEL` button in the Range tab pick row (works in planning too — same `PickSlot`/`voxelPickArmed` delivery as BLAST/BLDG). Click any voxel → HUD shows `cell / mat / volume`, a yellow `SetCustomCube` marker frames the exact cell (yaw-aware), and `Debug.Log` dumps the full report: hit point/normal/dist, raw packed value + flag bits, volume dims/vs/yaw, and all 6 face-neighbor materials + exposed-face count (answers 'is that speck a hole showing the next cell?'). `StAssetReader.GetMaterialName` added — palette names for 0–147.
- **SmoothNormal NaN fix (single dark faces on thin voxels)**: `SmoothNormal` now returns a zero vector when opposite neighbours cancel (1-voxel strands/sheets) instead of `normalize(0)` = NaN, and the side-face blend is skipped for a zero gradient (flat face normal) — `VoxelProxyRaymarch.shader` + legacy `MobSimVoxelRaymarch.compute`. Root cause: the `length(n) < 0.001` guard ran AFTER the normalize and `NaN < x` is false, so NaN fed `GetLighting` and `max(NaN, ambientFloor)` left the face at the ambient floor (flat dark). Evidence: Voxel Inspector log on the dark voxel showed `nb: -x:air +x:air -y:air +y:air -z:wood +z:wood` (gradient sum exactly 0); offline census — 6,400 of 116,186 side-exposed tenement voxels (5.5%) are degenerate pristine, r6m/p2/6-pass blasts add up to +10.6k. Checked: both functions compile under d3dcompiler_47; old disassembly = normalize (`rsq`) before the guard (`lt` 0.001), new = `movc` to the face normal. Confirmed by user playtest (`docs/known_issues/rendering/SMOOTH_NORMAL_NAN_THIN_VOXELS.md`, FIXED). Expected side effect: all degenerate faces everywhere now light like their neighbours.
- **Voxel Inspector v2 logging**: the report now adds inspect #/time/frame, camera pos+fwd, volume corner/extent/cell-center-local, a damage delta vs the pristine array (this cell + each of the 6 neighbours: `[was X - REMOVED/CHANGED]`, count changed by damage), a replica of the shader's shading inputs (face normal, neighbour-gradient sum, DEGENERATE flag, blended normal) and lighting (sun dir via new `VoxelChunkManager.GetLightDirection()`, N·L, half-Lambert² term, CPU shadow-ray replica through `VoxelCollisionWorld.Raycast` using the shader's skip/max steps + nudges → CLEAR / BLOCKED by cell+material, verdict). 2-line HUD summary. Every report is also appended to `Logs/voxel_inspect.log` (git-ignored; survives editor restarts — Editor.log is 20+ MB and the search tools skip files >4 MB). New helper: `VoxelDamageSystem.TryGetOriginalVoxel`.
- **.gitignore**: ignore the local working export `/VoxelAssetStudio/tenement_block_0.json` (46 MB `stasset_to_json.py` output of the production tenement, being hand-edited); anchored to the root path so the tracked `VoxelAssetStudio/JSON Models In Progress/tenement_block_0.json` is unaffected.
- **Glass monolith testbed**: `Assets/StreamingAssets/voxel_buildings/tenement_glass.stasset` fabricated via `stasset_io.save_stasset` — a SOLID 192×120×192-cell (9.6×6×9.6m at the runtime voxelSize 0.05 hard-set in CityMap3D; the asset JSON's 0.1 is metadata only) block of pure mat-112 window glass, 4,423,680 voxels. Wired to `block_47` in `city_layout.json` (one block east of the shooting range at block_46; multi-building empty-land slots skipped via full-block-dims path). Debug instrument: removed cavity = true damage field; crack shell (all in-radius glass flips 134) = candidate sphere → concentric readout of reach vs field strength. Glass self-shields ×0.95/cell → damage saturates ~76 cells (~3.8m at 0.05) deep regardless of power. Warning: r≥20m cracks all 4.4M cells (multi-second hitch); iterate at r3–8m. NOTE: `city_layout.json` is generated — re-pointing block_47 (and the block_48 debris lot) is reverted by `Tools/generate_city_layout.py` regen.
- **Voxel-aware debris + rubble deposit** (`ShardPhysics.cs`, new — pure, drives from `Tools/dda_selftest`): shards no longer fall to a flat `y=0` plane. Each airborne shard is a sphere (r = half its cube edge) swept along its velocity by ONE `VoxelCollisionWorld.Raycast` per frame (the DDA visits every crossed cell, so a 30 m/s shard cannot tunnel a 1-voxel floor); a hit stops it a radius off the face and reflects velocity about the face normal (restitution `shardBounce` 0.35, tangential ×0.6) — floors, roofs, walls and earlier rubble are one code path, only `hit.normal` differs. The flat plane stays as the fallback (collision off / nothing underneath). A shard embedded in solid fades in 0.15 s. **Rubble**: a shard that comes to rest on a floor-like face (normal.y > 0.7, |v| < 0.4) of a volume that already has damage state waits, then `ShardPhysics.Deposit` writes ITS OWN material (cell it was blasted from, or the majority material of an island chunk) into the AIR cells it settled on — never over solid, clipped to the grid — via one `UploadVoxelEdits` dirty-span batch. Dust (< 1.5 cells) = 1 cell on the first air layer above the surface; an island chunk deposits an n³ block (n = size/vs, cap `rubbleMaxCells` 20), so a collapsed wall section lands as a block, not confetti. Piles stack (the next shard lands on the last). **Gating**: deposits wait for the shock-front playback window (`planBusyUntil` = fuse + duration + 0.25 s) so rubble can never land on an island that is about to drop and hang in mid-air, and at deposit time the surface under the shard is re-probed — if it vanished the shard wakes and falls again (verified: 0 deposits before the islands drop, 3–4 wake-ups per blast). Max 32 deposits/frame. Terrain landings and untouched neighbour buildings just linger 1.1 s and fade (rubble only goes into volumes already damaged this session). HUD (Range tab, Demolition block): `debris bounces off voxels` + `debris settles into volume (rubble · N cells)` toggles — switch rubble off to keep the glass monolith a clean blast instrument. `RESET DAMAGE` clears rubble with the rest of the damage. Offline: self-test [13] (floor/thin-floor/wall/yawed-wall/embedded/skimming + 700-shard fuzz in a sealed room: 0 tunnelled, 177k bounces) and [14] (dust stacks 4→5→6, 4-cell chunk = 64-cell block, solid kept, bounds clipped) — ALL PASS (148,474 checks). Real-asset study (tenement, r6m/p2/16): legacy plane = every shard on y=0; sweep = shards rest on every story, rubble 190–300 cells per blast, 92–97% of rubble cells have solid directly below, 0 isolated floating cells. **Known limits**: rubble mass was bounded by `maxShards` — superseded by the mass-conserving entry below; the center ray lets a large chunk graze into a wall by up to its radius while moving parallel to it; rubble is not persisted (same as all damage — baking the [BOMBED] state is still open).
- **Mass-conserving debris** (rubble piles): blast-front / radial / island emission now runs `ShardPhysics.BuildBuckets` — removed cells spatial-hash into `debrisClusterM`-pitch buckets, ONE shard per bucket carrying the bucket's cell count as `mass`. In flight a shard renders as a uniform cube of edge ∛count **capped at 5 voxels** — never a true-AABB slab (render size ≠ deposit mass; the earlier "big cubes" regression came from drawing the bucket's real footprint). On rest `ShardPhysics.DepositHeap` spills `mass` cells into AIR cells around the rest cell as square shrinking layers (−1 half-extent every 2 layers, footprint cap `rubbleMaxCells`) — a mound, so rubble scales with what was removed rather than the 192-shard budget. Motion is size-graded (launch speed and tumble ÷ √edge). `PushShard` eviction prefers debris that can't deposit (flying / on terrain) so settled mass isn't silently dropped; a per-frame cell budget (65536) joins the 32-shard deposit cap. Bullet spall stays 1:1 (already mass-conserving). Selftest [15]: bucket mass/centroid/majority-vote/crack-filter + heap writes exact mass, stacks layers, never over solid, clips at the grid corner.
- **Budget-aware emission + mass accounting** (fixes "0 debris is actually made anywhere"): the first bucket pass produced ~15,000 shards per blast (sparse removed cells spread over `debrisClusterM` buckets) and `maxShards` evicted ~14,900 of them before they could deposit — a 423k-cell blast put back ~230 cells. Now the budget is explicit: blast fronts share ~70% of `maxShards` proportional to their AIR-cell counts, the island span gets ~30% as ONE bucket range, and `EmitClusterRange` auto-coarsens pitch until a range's bucket count fits its share — every removed cell lands in some bucket, so ~226 shards carry the whole blast. Each shard spawns at a real removed cell nearest its bucket centroid (`BuildBuckets` tracks `repIdx`; raw centroids sat inside surviving solid — 429 embedded starts on a center blast → 0). Mass rescue: a settled shard evicted over the budget spills its heap first; a shard embedded in solid after the collapse window spills its mass into the air around the wedge point. Instrumentation: `🧨 debris` log per detonation = emitted shards + carried cells + evicted cells; `🧱 rubble settling` = written/carried per deposit. Real-tenement study (r6/p2/16): center 423,130 removed → **157,464 rubble cells** (was ~230); south face 173,500 → 74,891; roof 302,002 → 159,084 — piles land on every story, 91–96% of rubble cells sit on solid, ≤1 isolated floater, shard loop peaks ≤5.1 ms.
- **Debris test lot** (`block_48` "[DEBRIS TESTBED]", next to the glass monolith): `Tools/gen_debris_lot.py` → `debris_test_lot.stasset` (192×120×192) — full-footprint asphalt pad (guaranteed rubble floor) + 72³ red-brick cube + thin stone wall + 30³ light-wood cube, one material each so pile color shows provenance; `blast_target*` attachment points for deterministic aim. `city_layout.json` block_48 collapsed to the single-building form — same caveat as block_47: a `generate_city_layout.py` regen reverts it (testbeds aren't in the 10x10 backup).
- **`SolidVolumeSet.Raycast` broadphase reject**: a six-compare segment-AABB test ahead of the slab test — 41.8 µs → 3.7 µs per short ray with 800 registered volumes (192 sweeping shards: 8.0 ms → 0.7 ms per frame). Exact (conservative box, same 1e-4 pad); the 108k-ray brute-force comparison and the yawed-volume checks still pass. Benefits bullets/LOS too.
- **Defaults**: `frontPasses` 16 (HUD recipe r6 m / p2 / 16 passes).
- **Tenement floor bands**: the floor-slab edge rungs (y 18-19, 34-35, 50-51, 66-67, 82-83) were painted metal (0.90 resistance — indestructible plates between floors); 36,704 plate cells → brick `100` (wall rate) using a thin-member filter (run ≥ 5 in both x and z) so 792 fire-escape rail/ladder cells sharing those layers stay 111. `tenement_block_0.stasset` regenerated (uncommitted); the local JSON export is git-ignored.
- **Isotropic occlusion (path-length march)**: `MarchTransmit` was charging `(1−res)` per solid cell ENTERED — a Manhattan-style metric, so 45° rays took ~1.41× the shielding and craters trended diamond/cross-shaped (measured: axis/diag reach 1.04–1.37; study image in %TEMP%/blast_study). Now Beer–Lambert: `transmit *= (1−res)^(ray length inside the cell)`, using the DDA's exact t-intervals — axis-aligned wall crossings cost exactly what they did (brick3 = 0.125, verified), oblique rays pay true distance. Public for selftest/debug. Selftest [12]: isotropy ln-ratio 1.01 + axis invariance. Side effect: oblique shielding no longer over-counts → slightly more damage at identical settings (+15–46% slab cells in the study) — power/radius may want a notch down. ALL PASS (148,444 checks).

## Glass puncture P2 — sector-baked merged-buffer writes — Oct 11

- **The gap was GPU-only**: `RegisterSectorPlacements` already registers collision volumes under the same `{blockId}_building[_N]` ids in both render modes, and `PlacedBuilding.voxels` is the same array COW clones — so `Puncture` + collision re-registration needed zero changes.
- `SectorBaker.PlacedBuilding` gains `bufferOffset` + `buildingIndex` (the index whose `<<16` forms the merged pane-id base).
- `CityMap3D.RegisterSectorPlacements` calls `chunkManager.RegisterSectorVolume(chunkName, sector, bufferOffset, buildingIndex, w, h, d)` per placement — a `sectorVolumeLookup` inside `VoxelChunkManager` maps volume id → (sector, offset, paneIdBase, dims).
- `VoxelChunkManager.UploadVoxelEdits`: falls through to the merged path when the name isn't a live chunk — same dirty-span `SetData`, just at `bufferOffset + localIndex` into `mergedVoxelBuffer` and `paneBuffer`. Sector damage pane ids use a fresh `0x8000_0000+` band (above every authored `b<<16 + localId`).
- `RefreshChunkVoxels` (damage reset): writes `st.original` into the merged region and rebuilds that region's pane ids with the building's id base.
- `UnregisterSector`/`ClearAllChunks` prune the volume→sector map; `VoxelDamageSystem` now gates on `HasWritableVolume` (chunk OR sector region).
- Verified: `tenement_block_0.stasset` has 4,608 cells of mat 112 + 1,792 of mat 114 (real windows — the hand-crafted asset carries glass already; `vehicle_civilian_car_0` has 40). Editor Assembly-CSharp compiles clean.
- 🧪 Playtest PASSED (Oct 11): sector baking ON, Vinny teleported to the street via PICK STAND, aimed at tenement storefront windows via PICK AIM — punctures + crack halos + shard bursts + SFX all render from the merged sector buffer, damage persists, reset restores.

## Glass puncture P1 dressing — shards + synthesized audio — Oct 11

### Shard burst (voxel → particle 1:1)
- `VoxelDamageSystem.EmitShards`: every **removed** cell (dirty idx → AIR) respawns as a shard at its world position via `cell → RotY(local − half)` around the volume center; velocity = `shotDir × shardSpeed` ±30% + lateral scatter + up bias; gravity + `shardLife` (0.7s ±30%).
- Rendered as velocity-stretched `PathDebugRenderer` custom beams (tiny elongated boxes, fade-out) — rides the instanced beam pipeline, no new render path. Capped at `maxShards` (192), oldest evict first; tint = hit material's palette color.

### Synthesized glass SFX — no audio assets
- `VoxelDamageSystem.GlassClip(removed)`: `AudioClip.Create` + `SetData` fills an in-memory buffer with **noise attack + inharmonic decaying partials + noise tail** (each layer × `exp(-k·t)` envelope). Buckets: ≤4 = tink (0.14s, 5200/7400 Hz), ≤15 = crack (0.28s, mid partials), >15 = crash (0.55s, low + heavy noise). Cached per bucket.
- `PlayClipAtPoint` at the hit point — positional 3D, `glassAudioVolume` × `clamp(0.4 + removed/20)`.
- **`ring1RemoveFraction` default → 0.2** (small hole + wide cracked halo — user-preferred look).

### AudioListener gap — the "no sound" root cause
- `CityMap3D` map camera: auto-adds an `AudioListener` when none exists in the scene — the planning-phase listener the game never had (MapCamera is created without one; FollowCamera's only appears with the working week).
- `FollowCamera`: disables every *other* listener when it takes over, avoiding the two-active-listeners warning.
- `VoxelDamageSystem.PreviewGlassSound(bucket)` + **`sfx test: tink/crack/crash` buttons** at the top of the Range tab (above the Dwelling gate — usable in planning): plays at the listener's position so distance can't mask a pipeline failure; logs listener name + clip length.

### Docs
- `docs/systems/PROCEDURAL_AUDIO.md` (new): the synthesis recipe, sound-design space (gunshot/ricochet/footstep…), `SynthRecipe` parameterization, and the HTML audio editor feasibility sketch (Web Audio API reproduces the math 1:1; JSON recipe → Unity synth mirrors the JSON↔stasset pipeline). Indexed in `DOCUMENTATION_INDEX.md`.

## October 11, 2026 (late) — Aim feel: sway + recoil spring + spread-cone wireframe

### The gun now kicks — and follow-up shots inherit the deflection
- **`AimFeelSpring`** (new, `AimFeelSpring.cs`): second-order spring on an angular aim offset (x = up, y = right). Equilibrium = wandering sway target; impulses = recoil. Semi-implicit Euler, gains derived from a desired settle time. Impulse while displaced → stacking wildness for free.
- **`ArmAimSolver`**: spring output rotates `TargetWorld` about the muzzle **before** the IK solve — torso + elbow chase the perturbed point, `WeaponMount.AimDirection` (and therefore the *next* shot) physically deflects and recovers. Skill-scaled: sway amplitude ×`SkillSpreadMultiplier`, settle ~0.3×/missing skill point. `ShotFired` subscription seeds the kick's lateral component off the shot seed.
- **`ProjectileSystem`**: new `ShotFired` event at trigger pull (the shot samples the pre-kick bore); `Hash01` now public; **spread-cone wireframe** — 6 edge rays + rim at `EffectiveConeDeg` around the live bore, dances with sway/recoil.
- **Range tab**: `spread cone` toggle, `aim feel` toggle + `sway ° @10` / `kick °` / `settle s @10` sliders, live `aim offset` readout; `feelSkill` mirrors the skill slider (placeholder for hood Firearms stat).
- Docs: `COMBAT_VEHICLE_DESIGN.md` §14 status note (aim channel implemented; flinch/raise/knockdown channels still pending).

### Verification
- Editor + Player-dev `Assembly-CSharp` compile clean (new file appended).
- 🧪 **Playtest (pending)**: `spread cone` shows a wireframe cone around the muzzle; `aim feel` makes the bore/cone visibly wander (skill 1 wanders wide, skill 10 tight); FIRE → arm kicks up, recovers in `settle s`; rapid FIRE stacks deflection — follow-up shots land where the kicked barrel points.
- **Anti-flail pass** (post-feedback): pos/vel hard clamps (`maxOffsetDeg` 11° default, the SteelTide `iMax` lesson), fixed 1/120 s substeps (explicit Euler on a stiff spring explodes on one hitchy frame), `dampingRatio` 0.8→1.05 (no visible ring), saturating impulse snap (stacking bounded), slower sway octaves (calm correction, not servo-jitter).

## October 11, 2026 (late) — Projectiles v1 (building DDA) + sector-mode address registry fix

### Firearms: a shot from Vinny's gun now flies and hits (`docs/systems/PROJECTILE_SYSTEM.md`)
- **`ProjectileSystem`** (new): FIRE spawns a physical projectile at `WeaponMount.MuzzleWorld` along the bore axis (`AimDirection` — the IK solver's real result, residual error included) perturbed by a **seeded cone** (`baseConeDeg × (1 + (10−skill)×0.75)`; skill 1–10 default **10**, base cone default 1.0°, speed 40 u/s). Each frame the covered segment is ray-cast, so fast bullets cannot tunnel. Resolves to a `ShotReport` (volume, voxel cell, material, normal, distance, flight time, lateral miss vs `firing_target`, IK aim err, seed). Tracer + impact tick via `PathDebugRenderer` beams.
- **`VoxelDda`** (new, pure Amanatides–Woo, no Unity types) + **`SolidVolumeSet`** (new, shared-array building volumes with AABB broadphase and optional yaw) + **`VoxelCollisionWorld.Raycast / RegisterVolume / UnregisterVolume / ClearVolumes`**: nearest hit across the terrain grid and every building volume. Conventions mirror the renderer (`x + y·w + z·w·h`, solid = `voxel & 0x1FF`, cell → `corner + i·vs`). Volumes **reference** the already-resident voxel arrays (no copies) — the same shared-asset/per-instance shape as GPU instancing.
- **Range tab → Firearm panel**: `skill (1-10)`, `cone ° @10`, `speed u/s`, effective-cone readout, **FIRE** (enabled only while Dwelling + IK AIM blend ≥ 0.98), last-shot report. (Trigger is a button for now.)
- Docs corrected: `COMBAT_VEHICLE_DESIGN.md` claimed buildings were already in `VoxelCollisionWorld` (only terrain was; `ProbeGround` is downward-only) — fixed; `COMBAT_AUTOBATTLE.md` gets an implementation-status note.

### FIX (regression from this session's sector-baking flip): sector mode left the building address registry empty
- **Symptom**: with `useSectorBaking` ON the Range tab showed `Points: none` / `firing_position: MISSING`, IK AIM was disabled and Target Practice fell back to the block centre. **Root cause**: `RegisterAddress` was only called by the per-building path; `TryGetBuildingPointLocal` reads nothing else. Latent since sector baking was added, exposed when the scene default was flipped ON (commit `3439191`) without auditing consumers — the earlier "test passed" covered rendering only. See `docs/known_issues/runtime/SECTOR_MODE_ADDRESS_REGISTRY_EMPTY.md`.
- **Fix**: `SectorBaker` exposes `SectorData.placements` (`PlacedBuilding`: placement + a *reference* to the array its sector region was filled from); `CityMap3D.RegisterSectorPlacements` registers addresses (same conventions as the per-building path) and collision volumes; the per-building path registers volumes too (`BuildingFootprint.data`). Rendering code paths are untouched (no shader changes; sector buffers/meta identical).

- **Offline self-test (new, in repo)**: `Tools/dda_selftest/run.sh` — compiles `VoxelDda` + `SolidVolumeSet` with the editor-bundled Roslyn/.NET 6 and runs **148,016 checks** (108k random rays vs exhaustive brute force incl. cells/t/normals, axis-aligned + edge cases, registry, yawed volumes vs an independent oriented-box reference, real shots through `shooting_range.json`): ALL PASS. Dead-centre shot → Red Brick bullseye cell (96,8,169); ≈ 1.7 µs per shot.
- Editor + Player-dev `Assembly-CSharp` compile clean with Unity's Roslyn (new files included).
- 🧪 **Playtest PASSED (Oct 11)**: Vinny at the firing range — IK AIM → FIRE → physical projectile visibly strikes the target board; sector placements register (addresses + collision volumes); range attachment points resolve in sector mode.

## October 11, 2026 (evening) — Glass double-blend root-caused and fixed; `BUILDING_INSTANCING` pragma regression corrected

### Glass darkened when the camera was outside the volume (FIX, user-verified 🧪)
- **Symptom**: uniform glass material + single-voxel-thick panes showed different apparent transparency depending on camera position — windows "cleared up" the instant the camera crossed into the tenement's proxy volume. `Debug Glass Layers` instrumentation showed identical layer counts on both sides — not an alpha or pane-count problem.
- **Root cause**: pass 2 (glass) ran under `Cull Off` with `ZWrite Off` + alpha blend. A camera outside the volume rasterizes **both near and far proxy faces**, so every pixel ran the identical glass march twice and blended each pane twice; inside the volume only far faces exist → single blend. The brightness pop happened exactly at the volume boundary.
- **Fix**: proxy cube winding corrected — 6 of 12 triangles had been inward-facing (harmless under `Cull Off`, fatal for selective culling) so all faces are now outward; `Cull Front` added to pass 2 only. Passes 0/1 untouched.
- **Verified 🧪**: windows consistent inside and outside the volume; no brightness pop crossing the boundary.

### `BUILDING_INSTANCING` pragma: single-keyword `multi_compile` knocked out instanced characters/vehicles (REGRESSION, fix applied — pending test 🧪)
- **What happened**: `#pragma multi_compile_local BUILDING_INSTANCING` was re-applied on all three passes as build-stripping insurance. The user's next test showed cars and characters invisible again (buildings, terrain, glass fine) — a clean A/B: same working tree, only the pragma changed.
- **Root cause**: a single-keyword `multi_compile` gets **no** "keyword disabled" variant (Unity manual: single-keyword `shader_feature` adds it automatically; `multi_compile` needs the `_` placeholder). Each pass compiled one program with `BUILDING_INSTANCING` always defined, so every instanced draw — groups included — ran the sector branch and read `_BuildingMeta`/`_BuildingPositions` slots group draws never fill. Chunks (non-instanced) and sectors were unaffected.
- **Fix**: `#pragma multi_compile_local _ BUILDING_INSTANCING` on all three passes — both variants always compiled/shipped; keyword-less materials get the group path, `sectorMaterial` the sector path.
- **Earlier hunt**: the stashed "tangled" shader carried the same single-keyword pragma on all three passes (never committed — `git log -S` finds no commit with it) and its symptoms match, so it is the probable true cause of the "second layer" in `SHARED_MATERIAL_INSTANCED_DRAWS.md` — unproven retroactively. The first standalone build's symptoms are consistent with it too (not recorded whether that build had the pragma).
- Full write-up: `docs/known_issues/rendering/MULTI_COMPILE_SINGLE_KEYWORD_ALWAYS_ON.md`.
- 🧪 Test: Editor Play — cars/Vinny/civilians render at correct positions, buildings/terrain/glass unchanged, no `_BuildingMeta` SRV warnings. Then a standalone build — the pragma exists for builds, so that is where it must be confirmed.

### Instrumentation kept
- `Debug Glass Layers` (`_GlassLayerVis`): black = no glass, cyan = 1 pane, green = 2, yellow = 3, red = 4+. Kept as an inspector debug tool — it correctly exonerated layer count as the darkening cause.

### Ray jitter: briefly re-grafted, reverted by design call
- `_RayJitter` was re-added to address distant sub-pixel windows (far opening < 1 pixel → rays land on frame voxels → window renders opaque). It worked — far windows became stippled see-through — but the stipple conflicted with the crisp voxel aesthetic, so it was rolled back. C# jitter plumbing (`useRayJitter`, per-frame `SetFloat`) was left bound to a uniform the shader no longer declared — removed in the cleanup pass (night entry above).

### Files
- `Assets/Resources/Shaders/VoxelProxyRaymarch.shader` — `Cull Front` on pass 2, `multi_compile_local _ BUILDING_INSTANCING` ×3, `_GlassLayerVis` debug path
- `Assets/Scripts/UI/VoxelChunkManager.cs` — proxy cube winding fix, `Debug Glass Layers` toggle

## October 11, 2026 (later) — Instanced invisibility root-caused; glass restored on the proven base

### The bug hunt (evidence-first, all findings verified)
- **Symptom**: instanced cars + characters invisible; scene gizmos at correct positions; CPU matrices valid (`nanMats=0`), draws emitted with `instancing=True`.
- **Diagnostic trap**: the first rasterization probe wrote `depth = 0.0` — farthest value under D3D12 reversed-Z, so every city fragment legally buried it. "No magenta" never meant "no draw".
- **Root cause 1 — shared `proxyMaterial`**: one material served both non-instanced chunk `DrawMesh` (hundreds/frame) and instanced group `DrawMeshInstanced` — per-draw instancing constant-buffer state got clobbered between draws; instances read stale matrices (the "displaced north up the street" sighting). **Fix**: dedicated `groupMaterial` (same pattern as `sectorMaterial`) with per-frame `CopyPropertiesFromMaterial`. Rasterization + correct positions restored immediately.
- **Root cause 2 — tangled shader diff**: even with delivery fixed, the accumulated uncommitted shader (glass + occupancy-DDA + jitter + iterVis + sitting-pose + CGINCLUDE refactor + probes) marched nothing at instanced offsets. Rather than keep patching, the shader was **reverted to last-known-good HEAD**; instancing confirmed working, then glass was **re-grafted incrementally**:
  - `CGINCLUDE` + 3-pass structure alone → instancing fine (structure exonerated).
  - Glass set grafted: `_PaneIDs`, `_GlassForcedMat0-7`, `_FlaggedGlassAlpha`, `_GlassSpecular`, `_GlassMaxLayers`, `IsGlassVoxel`/`GlassLit`/`AdvanceDDA`, front-to-back accumulation + frosted-pane terminal, nearest-surface depth, passes 0/1/2.
  - **Deliberately excluded** (per scope call — the FPS hunt they belonged to was already resolved by sector baking): `_OccupancyData`/`BrickOccupied`/`BrickExitT`, `_RayJitter`, `_IterVis`, debug probes, sitting-pose anim changes (those were also the Vinny walk-speed regression — HEAD's anim path restored normal speed).
  - ⚠️ The October 11 entry above documents occupancy/jitter/iterVis as if current — those features were **removed from the shader** here; the C# build/bind scaffolding stayed as dead work until the cleanup pass (night entry above) removed it.

### Sector two-pass composite (buildings join cars on the full glass pipeline)
- Sectors hardcoded pass 0 ("far LOD legacy path") — restored to composite: opaque draw emits `useGlassCompositePass ? 1 : 0`, glass pass-2 emitted far→near per sector.
- `BakedSector.paneBuffer`: per-building pane flood-fill scattered into a merged array in `mergedVoxelData` index space; per-building ids offset `b<<16` to prevent cross-building `lastPane` collisions.
- `_PaneMerged` shader flag: sectors index `_PaneIDs[bufferOffset + VoxelIndex]`; chunks/groups keep asset-local indexing. Pass-2 emission gated on `paneBuffer != default` → glass-free sectors cost nothing.
- `useGlassCompositePass` default **false → true**.

### Files
- `Assets/Scripts/UI/VoxelChunkManager.cs` — `groupMaterial`, composite default, sector pane build/bind/release/pass-2, `propPaneMerged`
- `Assets/Resources/Shaders/VoxelProxyRaymarch.shader` — HEAD + glass graft + `_PaneMerged`
- `docs/known_issues/rendering/SHARED_MATERIAL_INSTANCED_DRAWS.md` — full diagnostic trail + reversed-Z probe lesson

### Verified by user 🧪
- Instanced cars at road positions + Vinny walking at normal speed.
- Car glass composites (two-pass); `useGlassCompositePass` toggle ON by default.
- Sector buildings on the same two-pass pipeline.

### Stash status
- Superseded: the removed occupancy/jitter/iterVis/sitting-pose shader work is archived as branch `archive/occupancy-dda`; the stash was dropped in the cleanup pass (night entry above).

## October 11, 2026 — Perf: 120→40 FPS regression traced, fixed, and a structural raymarch win

> **Superseded (2026-10-11 night)**: occupancy skip and ray jitter were later shelved and removed; the heatmap was restored behind a shader keyword. See the top entries and `docs/core/PERFORMANCE_PATTERNS.md` #13-#14.

### The investigation (all findings measured)
- Regression: ~120 → ~40 FPS before the working week and before the glass composite toggle. WASD/F6 camera suspected by timing — **exonerated** (nanoseconds per frame).
- Profiler truth: `Scripts` total **0.98ms**, `VoxelChunkManager` **1.5ms** (0.73 cull + 0.75 draw, 788 registered / 64 drawn) — renderer innocent. `DebugHUDManager.OnGUI` topped GC contributors; the real cost billed to **EditorLoop/Others**.
- **Cause 1 — IMGUI per-event tax**: `OnGUI` runs once per event (Layout + Repaint + input ≈ 4–6×/frame). The HUD rebuilt ~60 widgets AND ran 8 `FindFirstObjectByType` scans on every event. A/B: **32 FPS open → 120 FPS minimized**.
- **Cause 2 — angle-dependent march depth (GPU, ~11ms)**: top-down rays hit roofs in a few DDA steps; angled views march through mostly-air interiors at voxel granularity.

### Fix 1 — `DebugHUDManager.cs`
- Text-only tabs (Camera/Render/Path/Keys) rebuild into ONE cached rich-text string at ~4 Hz → single `GUILayout.Label` per repaint instead of ~40 widgets × ~5 events/frame. Interactive tabs (Clothing buttons, Range sliders) stay live.
- `RefreshReferences` (8 scene scans) throttled to 0.5s; footer strings cached likewise. Visuals preserved — headers carried via inline rich-text color.

### Fix 2 — two-level DDA brick occupancy skip (`useOccupancySkip`, default ON)
- `_OccupancyData`: one bit per 8³ brick, set iff the brick holds any non-air voxel. The march tests occupancy **once per entered brick**; all-air bricks cross in one boundary jump (`BrickExitT` + `ResyncSideDist`) instead of ~21 cell iterations. Same skip in the **shadow march**.
- `VoxelChunkManager`: `GetOccupancy`/`ComputeOccupancy`/`DilateOccupancy` at registration, cached by packed-array ref (siblings of the pane-ID cache); ~1 bit per 64 voxels. Wired for chunks, instanced groups, and merged sectors — sector buildings each carry a word offset into the concatenated bitmask via `_BuildingOccOffsets` (rides the unused `yaw` varying).
- Group-animated assets get a **1-brick dilation** so posed limbs can't overstep rest-air bricks. Literature match: MultiDDA/two-level grids show ~4.7× ray throughput on sparse-interior volumes.

### Fix 3 — sub-pixel ray jitter (`useRayJitter`, default ON, ±0.5px)
- `_RayJitter` world-units lateral origin dither (static hash per pixel → stable stipple). C# computes one render pixel's world size per frame (exact in ortho; median drawn distance in perspective). Fixes the earlier window-through-window drop: aligned window bores narrower than the pixel grid now get sampled.

### Files
- `Assets/Resources/Shaders/VoxelProxyRaymarch.shader` — `_OccupancyData`/`_HasOccupancy`/`_BuildingOccOffsets`/`_RayJitter` bindings, `BrickOccupied`/`BrickExitT`/`ResyncSideDist` helpers, skip in primary + shadow marches, jitter block, occupancy offset on building `yaw`.
- `Assets/Scripts/UI/VoxelChunkManager.cs` — occupancy build/cache/bind/release at all registration sites, `_HasOccupancy` per-draw flag, `_RayJitter` amplitude per frame, new inspector fields (`useOccupancySkip`, `useRayJitter`, `rayJitterPixels`).
- `Assets/Scripts/Sim/DebugHUDManager.cs` — cached tab/footer strings, throttled reference scans.
- `docs/core/PERFORMANCE_PATTERNS.md` — pattern 13 (two-level DDA) + cross-cutting lesson 6 (IMGUI per-event billing).
- `docs/known_issues/rendering/IMGUI_HUD_FRAME_TAX.md` — full diagnostic trail + exonerated suspects.

### Expected behavior 🧪
- Debug HUD open should no longer tank the frame rate (was 32 open / 120 minimized).
- Angled views should gain substantially vs. before (interior air now jumps 8 voxels per iteration); top-down unchanged.
- `useOccupancySkip` OFF returns the flat DDA — A/B checkpoint for correctness: the picture must be **pixel-identical** at the same camera (except intentional ±0.5px jitter stipple; toggle `useRayJitter` off for a strict diff).
- Watch for missing content at volume edges or posed characters (dilation guard covers limbs — report any limb tips vanishing at angle).

### Follow-up — grazing-angle drop isolated to per-building chunk count + debug tools
- User A/B confirmed: HUD fix verified (120 FPS with panel open, was 32). Angle drop persists → root cause identified: **`useSectorBaking = false`** means every building loads as its own chunk (`LoadChunkCentered`) — ~780 per-building volumes drawing 1:1. Terrain is already sector-baked (the 5 HUD sectors), characters instanced — only buildings go unbatched.
- Mechanism: proxy shader writes depth manually → no early-Z → at ~5° pitch a dozen building AABBs stack on the same pixels → N full-volume marches per pixel (overdraw), which occupancy can't reduce — the ray marches EVERY overlapping volume, not one.
- New diagnostics: `iterVisMode` inspector dropdown → per-pixel iteration heatmap with three views — `IterationHeat` (blue <80 / green ~80-160 / red >160 total march+shadow iters; misses render instead of discarding so air-march cost is visible), `StepChannels` (R=voxel-DDA steps, G=brick-jumps, B=shadow steps — green-dominant = occupancy skipping, red = cell crawling), `MissWaste` (heat only on no-hit rays — pure wasted march; hits render black). `PerfOccDraws` counter → HUD Chunks line shows `occ:N` submissions running with `_HasOccupancy=1` (0 = binding bug, N = skip engaged).
- First heatmap reading: tenement roofs, river channel, and shooting-range asset glow red → **sub-brick air crawling** — an 8³ brick holding a slab + air reads "occupied" so a ray skimming the air portion voxel-DDAs the whole length. StepChannels confirmed: fire escapes/range walls/lot debris render pure red (voxel steps, no jumps); roads render black (fast hits) ✓ occupancy working, granularity too coarse.
- Fix applied: **bricks shrunk 8³ → 4³** (`BRICK_SHIFT 2`, `BRICK_SIZE 4` in shader; `shift=2` in `ComputeOccupancy`). A thin slab now poisons only the 4-voxel band it sits in — air above becomes free bricks → ~4× less crawl surface + the mixed slab/air boundary narrows. Map memory 8× up but still trivial (~8.6KB per 4.4M-voxel building). Animated-group dilation radius 1→2 bricks to keep the ~8-voxel limb margin. If StepChannels still shows red on ultra-thin structures (1-voxel railings poison any brick size), next rung is a distance map or sector-baking removing the overdraw multiplier.
- 🧪 Test: flip `useSectorBaking` on CityMap3D → buildings collapse into sector draws (occupancy keeps working via per-instance word offsets) → grazing overdraw should collapse with it. Then `debugIterVis` on at the same camera → red zones should shrink to the buildings themselves.

### Follow-up — D3D12 standalone build: `_BuildingMeta` SRV error + invisible instanced cars/characters
- First dev build surfaced `d3d12: ... requires a buffer (SRV) "_BuildingMeta" ... Skipping draw calls`. Buildings/terrain rendered; instanced cars + Vinny spawned (registered, buffers initialized, `RenderInstancedGroup` invoked) but drew nothing. D3D12 validates every SRV the active variant references per draw; D3D11 tolerated gaps.
- **Root cause (two confirmed layers + one unconfirmed risk)**: (a) `_BuildingMeta`/`_BuildingPositions` bound only in the sector path — missing everywhere else; (b) `shader_feature_local` could have let the build strip the runtime-enabled `BUILDING_INSTANCING` variant — a plausible precautionary risk, never confirmed (sectors rendered fine in that same build); (c) five `ComputeBuffer`s lazily allocated **inside `RenderInstancedGroup`** (which runs inside `endCameraRendering`) — mid-render GPU-resource creation drops recorded D3D12 SRV bindings. Log correlation: the error fired only on frames with `Posed voxel buffer allocated`.
- **Fix**: `EnsureInstancedBuffers()` — a `LateUpdate` hook pre-sizes `instanceOffsetBuffer`, `posedVoxelBuffer`, `instanceAnimDataBuffer`, `instanceIKDataBuffer`, `instanceMaterialRemapBuffer` at `instances.Count` capacity (upper bound of `visibleCount`) before the render callback. In-render lazy branches kept as cold fallbacks. Plus `?? sharedMaterialBuffer` dummies on all three draw paths + `multi_compile_local BUILDING_INSTANCING` (user change, single keyword — **later found to be a regression**: no "off" variant, so instanced groups ran the sector branch; corrected to `multi_compile_local _ BUILDING_INSTANCING`, see entry above). The buffer pre-sizing remains valid hardening, but it may not have been the sole cause of the invisible cars in that build.
- Debug keys now work in builds: `V` cycles iterVisMode, `F11` occupancy, `F12` jitter.
- 🧪 Test (dev build): no SRV warnings, cars/Vinny render, sectors still render; verify `V`/`F11`/`F12`/`R` toggles.
